- The fixed official HTTPS Database endpoint, redirect prohibition, 2 MiB complete-payload limit, schema 2 envelope, technicalReport schema 3, lowercase 64-hex accepted report-id handling, and explicit user initiation remain unchanged.
- VulkanScope Database 0.39.9 compatibility must be verified for the current/future schema-compatible producer contract without requiring a D1 migration or a Database schema change.
- All 0.41.11 canonical-mask, profile UNKNOWN, VkToolPurposeFlags, format-mask and Image Format Properties2 tuple-state semantics remain unchanged.


## Release 0.41.13 Database query-group state requirements

- Application version is 0.41.13 with versionCode 423.
- Image Format Properties2 query-group completion remains governed by the project-wide complete-report rule: an explicitly `Unavailable` or `Not applicable` isolated query is complete query evidence and must not be replaced with fabricated tuple results.
- When the `imageFormat2` isolated group is not Available, any previous/inherited `imageFormatQueryResults` dataset must be cleared before the completed report is published. Stale tuple evidence must never survive across a failed or non-applicable collection cycle.
- technicalReport schema 3 adds `imageFormatQueryStatus` and `imageFormatQueryReason` per device as redundant structured provenance derived from the existing exact `Vulkan Query Status / Image Format Properties 2 query` evidence. These fields do not replace, hide or reclassify the canonical query-status row.
- `imageFormatQueryStatus` is `available`, `unavailable`, `not_applicable`, or `unknown`; the reason remains bounded source evidence and is empty for Available.
- Database submission remains schema 2 / technicalReport 3, fixed official HTTPS endpoint, no redirects, explicit opt-in and a 2 MiB fail-closed complete-payload bound. No report evidence may be truncated or omitted to obtain server acceptance.
- Vulkan 1.4.360, API 37, NDK r29, AGP 9.3.2, Gradle 9.7.1, three release ABIs and all 0.41.12 submission reliability invariants remain unchanged.


## Release 0.41.14 Kotlin compile-correctness requirements

- Application version is 0.41.14 with versionCode 424.
- The 0.41.13 Image Format Properties2 query-group state and Database submission contract remains functionally unchanged.
- Helper functions consuming the collected physical-device model must use the actual `DeviceReport` type declared by the application. Referencing nonexistent or stale model names such as `GpuInfo` is forbidden.
- The compile fix must not alter `detailedProperties`, Image Format Properties2 query-state derivation, technicalReport schema 3 fields, Database payload contents, submission endpoint, redirect policy, size limits, capability-state semantics, Vulkan query coverage, Turnip behavior, or ABI support.
- Release verification must fail if the stale `GpuInfo` helper signature reappears or if `imageFormatQueryGroupState` no longer accepts `DeviceReport`.
- Vulkan 1.4.360, API 37, NDK r29, AGP 9.3.2, Gradle 9.7.1, schema 2 / technicalReport 3, and the three release ABIs remain unchanged.

## Release 0.41.15 canonical enumeration and Android HDR provenance requirements
- Application version is 0.41.15 with versionCode 425. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- Every currently registered `VkPresentModeKHR` value handled by the application uses its exact canonical Khronos name, including shared-demand refresh, shared-continuous refresh and FIFO latest-ready. Unknown future present-mode values remain raw numeric evidence and must not be formatted as invented `VK_PRESENT_MODE_*` symbols.
- Unknown future `VkColorSpaceKHR` and `VkFormat` numeric values remain raw unknown evidence and must not be formatted as invented official-looking `VK_COLOR_SPACE_*` or `VK_FORMAT_*` names. Runtime-enumerated extension names remain exact.
- Instance-extension and instance-layer enumeration preserve query outcome, bounded `VK_INCOMPLETE` partial evidence and completion state. A failed enumeration must never be serialized as a successful empty list or marked complete.
- `VK_INCOMPLETE` remains partial positive evidence. Partial instance extension/layer entries may be retained, but missing entries cannot be inferred unsupported until enumeration completed successfully.
- A failed `vkEnumeratePhysicalDevices` call is Unavailable and retains the returned VkResult evidence. Not applicable is used only after a successful enumeration proves that no physical Vulkan device exists for the attempted query group.
- Extension-specific query groups may conclude Not applicable from a missing runtime extension only when the relevant device-extension enumeration completed successfully. If enumeration is incomplete or unavailable, the group remains Unavailable/Unknown instead of fabricating absence.
- On Android 14/API 34 and newer, the active `Display.Mode.supportedHdrTypes` list is authoritative for the current mode: a non-empty list is Available and an empty list is Unavailable. On API 24-33, a null `Display.hdrCapabilities` object is Unknown/not exposed, while a non-null object with an empty supported-HDR-types list is Unavailable.
- HDR capability status and exact HDR type evidence remain separate from Vulkan surface color-space evidence and flow through UI, Analysis, TXT, HTML and structured technicalReport without changing schemaVersion.
- Existing complete-report gating, fixed Database endpoint, report-size bounds, update/Turnip security, three-ABI policy and capability-state distinctions must not regress.

## Release 0.41.16 native/Kotlin compile-correctness requirements
- Application version is 0.41.16 with versionCode 426. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- `DeviceExtensionEnumeration.status` is a C string and must be compared by string contents, never by pointer identity.
- The 0.41.15 extension-enumeration uncertainty semantics remain unchanged; a non-available status continues to mean uncertain enumeration evidence and must retain its bounded reason.
- Shared HDR report-formatting helpers used by top-level TXT/HTML serializers must be declared at top level rather than as inaccessible `MainActivity` members.
- The Android HDR null-versus-empty semantics introduced in 0.41.15 remain unchanged.
- The release verifier must reject regression to direct C-string literal pointer comparison and must gate the shared HDR helper at top-level serializer scope.
- No compile-only correction may change Vulkan query results, capability-state semantics, Database payload schema, ABI coverage, update security, Turnip handling, privacy or report completeness.

## Release 0.41.17 native warning-clean compile requirements
- Application version is 0.41.17 with versionCode 427. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- Native release builds use `-Werror`; helper functions left unreachable after enumeration refactors are release-blocking defects and must be removed instead of suppressed with warning pragmas or attributes.
- The obsolete `instanceLayers(VulkanApi&)` vector-only wrapper must not exist; all current layer enumeration consumers use `enumerateInstanceLayers` so status/reason/completeness evidence remains authoritative and no dead compatibility wrapper is retained.
- 0.41.15 enumeration/HDR semantics and 0.41.16 string-comparison/Kotlin-scope corrections remain unchanged.
- The release verifier must reject reintroduction of the obsolete unused `instanceLayers(VulkanApi&)` wrapper.
- Compile-only correction must not change Vulkan query results, capability-state semantics, Database payload schema, ABI coverage, update security, Turnip handling, privacy or report completeness.

## Release 0.41.18 full correctness, provenance and report-integrity requirements
- Application version is 0.41.18 with versionCode 428. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- A native base checkpoint is final only when `baseReportComplete=true` or when the probe explicitly returns an Unavailable result. Numerical coverage heuristics and legacy readiness hints must never promote a partial checkpoint into a complete report.
- The native crash-marker file is considered positive only after the signal handler writes crash evidence. Merely creating the zero-length marker at probe startup is not a crash signal, and a crash must never cause the latest partial base checkpoint to be published as a complete report.
- Instance-layer extensions, device-layer enumeration and device-layer extensions preserve bounded `VK_INCOMPLETE` partial positive evidence together with explicit status, reason and completeness. Query failure must never collapse into a successful empty list.
- Per-layer extension-enumeration provenance and per-device layer-enumeration provenance flow through the application model, Vulkan UI, Analysis snapshot, TXT, HTML and technicalReport without reclassifying partial evidence as absence.
- Surface collection uses the status-bearing instance-extension enumeration. A missing required WSI extension is Not applicable only after complete enumeration proves absence; incomplete/unavailable enumeration remains Unavailable. Surface query status and reason are explicit report fields and are not inferred from an empty format or present-mode list.
- Metadata and Surface orchestration failures replace the affected query state with explicit Unavailable evidence for the current collection cycle. A previous successful Surface or metadata state must not silently survive a failed refresh as though it were current evidence.
- Unknown Vulkan enum/index fallback text must not imitate a registered `VK_*` token. Known current values use exact canonical Khronos names; unknown future/raw values remain visibly raw/unknown evidence.
- Release builds remain `-Werror` for project native code. Dead helpers, unsafe second-stage count growth and source-level warning suppressions are forbidden; bounded count/capacity checks remain mandatory.
- Native Vulkan and Android Surface ownership remains deterministic, probe result payloads remain bounded, cleartext traffic remains disabled, imported-driver paths remain confined to application-private storage, and no automatic/background Database submission is introduced.
- Large report collections remain lazy/searchable in UI and report serialization must preserve complete evidence rather than truncating or filtering data to improve performance or Database acceptance.
- VulkanScope Database 0.39.11 remains the compatible companion because the 0.41.18 technicalReport additions are provenance-only fields inside schema 3; no D1 migration, report-hash rewrite or submission-envelope schema change is required.

## Release 0.41.19 collection serialization, report preservation and package hygiene requirements
- Application version is 0.41.19 with versionCode 429. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- All isolated Vulkan service probes share one application-side coroutine mutex so timeout accounting begins only after the probe owns the single native worker slot; queued probes must not expire solely because an earlier probe is still running.
- A timed-out probe may stop the isolated process only while it owns that serialized probe slot, preventing one timeout from terminating unrelated queued collection work.
- Surface refresh results merge into the latest report for the same collection generation and must never overwrite newer feature/property/extension evidence with a stale pre-refresh snapshot.
- A new full collection must not clear or supersede still-pending ad-hoc or Surface collection tasks; pending recollection is started after those tasks finish.
- Third-party comparison-product names are prohibited throughout the shipped source tree, runtime evidence labels, verifier messages, audit filenames and packaged metadata. Historical validated query coverage remains available under neutral internal naming and Khronos registry/specification data remains authoritative.
- Source-release hygiene rejects README.md and release.md at any directory depth, any fastlane directory, Python cache files and stale source backups.
- Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14 remains the current Khronos published registry baseline verified for this release on 2026-08-27.

## Release 0.41.20 full reporting, registry-reference and stale-state correctness requirements
- Application version is 0.41.20 with versionCode 430. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- Loader API, base-probe instance API and physical-device API are separate evidence values. UI, Analysis and structured technical reports must not collapse them into one value or substitute the loader version for the instance version.
- Physical-device-group results emitted by the native `groups` probe must be retained in the application report instead of being discarded at the Kotlin merge boundary. `VK_INCOMPLETE` retains bounded partial `groupProperties`, the exact numeric enumeration result and `groupEnumerationComplete=false`; an incomplete group set must never be serialized as a successful empty result.
- Physical-device-group reporting is instance-level evidence. Per-device rows must not invent placeholder group-membership claims; actual returned group membership belongs in the retained `VkPhysicalDeviceGroupProperties` evidence.
- Surface reports retain the selected Surface-probe instance API, the exact Surface-format query entry point used, the returned format count and canonical VkResult names where known. Unknown future VkResult values remain explicit raw unknown evidence.
- Query-safety rejection booleans and Surface VkResult canonical text must remain first-class structured technical-report evidence as well as UI/TXT/HTML evidence; raw numeric values remain preserved alongside canonical text.
- Ad-hoc page queries must not run against a base report while the full collection still owns the report generation; otherwise later enrichment could overwrite their results. Surface changes observed during full collection must schedule a subsequent current-state recollection rather than being silently dropped.
- Changing driver mode always schedules a fresh Vulkan collection even if no current Android Surface is available, preventing a new driver label from being paired with stale evidence from the previous driver. Every collection, Surface query and ad-hoc probe snapshots its driver mode; changing mode clears the old report and results from an older mode must not be published afterward.
- Profile extension absence is FAIL/MISSING only when the authoritative enumeration for that extension scope completed successfully. Instance-scoped extensions use instance enumeration certainty; device-scoped extensions use selected-device enumeration certainty.
- The Android 16 lightweight requirements evaluator includes the directly verified r.7 MUST feature set and safely comparable integer/boolean property subset, but remains explicitly incomplete and therefore must not claim full Khronos/Android profile PASS.
- Known `VkPhysicalDeviceType` values use exact canonical `VK_PHYSICAL_DEVICE_TYPE_*` names. Friendly GPU-type marketing labels must not replace registered enum names; unknown future values retain raw numeric evidence.
- Vulkan API minimum gates compare major and minor version together; minor-only comparisons are prohibited because a future higher major version must not be misclassified as older than Vulkan 1.x.
- The checked-in extension-reference asset is a supplementary subset, not a complete Vulkan Registry census. Empty metadata fields are Unavailable and must never be interpreted as proof that an extension has no platform, promotion, dependency, deprecation, obsoletion or other registry metadata.
- The extension-reference subset is derived from one checked-in map; duplicate hand-maintained extension-name catalogs are prohibited. Runtime extension support remains based only on runtime enumeration.
- Registry audit manifest/snapshot counts must match the native query catalog. Stale 31/296 audit counts and the misnamed `runtimeExtensionTokens` list are prohibited; the 0.41.20 checked-in native catalog records 109 implemented physical-device structs, 104 validated runtime query groups and 268 runtime registry-token references.
- Lazy/background-query exceptions and blank results must leave explicit Unavailable evidence through the normal merge path instead of completing a collection task with no report state.
- The corrected internal registry metric name is `runtimeRegistryTokenReferenceCount`; `runtimeExtensionTokenCount` may exist only as a technicalReport/backward-reader compatibility alias and must not be presented as the canonical field name.
- Existing complete-report, privacy, update/download, Turnip path-confinement, probe-result bounds, isolated-process, three-ABI, cleartext-disabled, source-comment and package-hygiene requirements remain mandatory.
- Git dependencies pinned by commit hash must use the full immutable commit id and must not enable CMake `GIT_SHALLOW`; the pinned libadrenotools source remains commit `8fae8ce254dfc1344527e05301e43f37dea2df80` with recursive submodules and non-shallow fetch semantics.

## Release 0.41.21 Surface, external-capability and evidence-state correctness requirements
- Application version is 0.41.21 with versionCode 431. Vulkan published/query and compile-header baseline remains 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- The base Vulkan collector does not create, own or query an Android `VkSurfaceKHR`. All real Android Surface/WSI work belongs exclusively to the isolated live-Surface probe; unreachable duplicate base-Surface code is prohibited.
- `VK_EXT_swapchain_colorspace` absence is reported as Not exposed only after complete instance-extension enumeration proves absence. If enumeration is incomplete or unavailable, the state is Unknown; positive partial enumeration may still prove availability.
- Every `vkGetPhysicalDeviceSurfaceSupportKHR` call retains its exact numeric `VkResult`. A failed presentation-support query must never be rendered or serialized as `NO PRESENT` / Not supported merely because its output boolean remained false.
- Surface query status is Available only when bounded queue-family presentation queries, applicable capability query, surface-format enumeration and present-mode enumeration all complete successfully. Partial positive evidence remains retained under explicit Incomplete status and bounded reason text.
- Non-null-Surface dependent WSI queries obey Vulkan valid usage: `vkGetPhysicalDeviceSurfaceCapabilities*`, `vkGetPhysicalDeviceSurfaceFormats*` and `vkGetPhysicalDeviceSurfacePresentModesKHR` are attempted only after at least one queue family has returned `VK_SUCCESS` with presentation support for that physical device and Surface. If complete presentation enumeration proves no supporting queue, dependent WSI queries are Not applicable; if support could not be proven because presentation queries are incomplete, dependent WSI state is Unknown and those commands are not called.
- Surface completeness also requires complete instance-extension enumeration so unknown optional WSI extension exposure cannot be silently promoted to a complete Surface snapshot.
- A Surface query that was not attempted must never serialize a compatibility sentinel such as `-1` as a real Vulkan error. Structured output uses explicit attempted/status fields and null/Not attempted canonical evidence for absent query results.
- External buffer, fence and semaphore capability paths are independent. A missing entry point for one path must not discard valid evidence from another path; each unavailable entry point leaves explicit Unavailable evidence.
- External-handle report names use exact Vulkan handle-type tokens for the queried handle. `VK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_FD_BIT`, `VK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY_HANDLE_TYPE_DMA_BUF_BIT_EXT`, `VK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY_HANDLE_TYPE_ANDROID_HARDWARE_BUFFER_BIT_ANDROID`, `VK_EXTERNAL_FENCE_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_FD_BIT`, `VK_EXTERNAL_FENCE_HANDLE_TYPE_SYNC_FD_BIT`, `VK_EXTERNAL_SEMAPHORE_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_FD_BIT` and `VK_EXTERNAL_SEMAPHORE_HANDLE_TYPE_SYNC_FD_BIT` must not be replaced by misleading friendly aliases. Applicable FD fence/semaphore paths query both OPAQUE_FD and SYNC_FD independently; applicable external-memory paths preserve OPAQUE_FD, DMA_BUF and Android Hardware Buffer evidence independently.
- External-capability prerequisite absence is Not applicable only when complete device-extension enumeration proves the corresponding extension absent. Incomplete/unavailable extension enumeration leaves Unknown evidence instead of inferring absence.
- Isolated query instances preserve Vulkan 1.0 extension semantics: when advertised, `VK_KHR_device_group_creation`, `VK_KHR_external_memory_capabilities`, `VK_KHR_external_fence_capabilities` and `VK_KHR_external_semaphore_capabilities` are enabled alongside `VK_KHR_get_physical_device_properties2`. `vkEnumeratePhysicalDeviceGroupsKHR` is a required fallback for Vulkan 1.0 stacks exposing device-group creation. Promoted core commands must not be assumed usable on a 1.0 instance without their providing instance extension.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceFormatProperties2` is a void query and must be called directly after its entry-point availability gate. Comma-operator pseudo-success conditions or other synthetic success predicates are prohibited.
- Android display properties distinguish platform-API unavailability, unavailable/unknown capability objects and proven absence. APIs unavailable on an older Android release must not be reported as a hardware/display capability being Not exposed.
- UI, Analysis snapshots, TXT, HTML and schema-3 technicalReport preserve the new Surface color-space-extension state and per-queue presentation `VkResult` evidence without removing existing raw booleans or compatibility fields.
- Existing complete-report, stale-generation, driver-snapshot, privacy, update/download, Database no-redirect/all-or-nothing, Turnip path-confinement, JNI/native ownership, query bounds, three-ABI, source-comment and package-hygiene requirements remain mandatory.
- Repeated `VK_INCOMPLETE` from `vkEnumeratePhysicalDevices` preserves bounded returned physical-device handles as partial positive evidence. Those devices may be displayed with exact enumeration `VkResult` and completeness provenance, but the base report remains incomplete and cannot enable TXT/HTML export or Database submission until physical-device enumeration completes with `VK_SUCCESS`.
- Any query group that receives bounded partial physical-device enumeration may retain positive evidence for returned devices under explicit Incomplete status. It must not infer global device/API/extension absence or return Not applicable from a device set known to be incomplete.
- Isolated-process evidence must not be attributed by a non-unique `vendorId`/`deviceId` pair. If the base report or isolated result contains multiple physical devices with the same pair and no stable cross-process identity is available, Surface, advanced, extension and self-test evidence is Unavailable for that ambiguous identity rather than being assigned to the first matching device.
- An advertised `VK_KHR_get_surface_capabilities2` with unavailable required entry points does not invalidate otherwise usable classic `VK_KHR_surface` evidence. The live-Surface probe falls back to the classic capability/format path and records the capabilities2 path as unavailable provenance.
- Kotlin merge code must not contain duplicate local declarations that make the release uncompilable; release verification gates the canonical isolated-query status declarations and the physical-device evidence merge invariants.


## Release 0.41.22 full re-audit, multi-device and query-state correctness requirements
- Application version is 0.41.22 with versionCode 432. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- A successful Android HDR-type query returning an empty list is valid negative capability evidence, not an unavailable query. UI, TXT, HTML, Analysis and structured display evidence distinguish Available with no reported HDR types, Unknown, and Unavailable without collapsing those states.
- Vulkan API capability gates compare major and minor versions together. Direct minor-only capability gating outside the canonical version-formatting/comparison helpers is prohibited so a future higher Vulkan major version cannot be misclassified as older than Vulkan 1.x.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoFormatPropertiesKHR` preserves `VK_SUCCESS` with zero matching formats as a completed zero-result query. `VK_INCOMPLETE` is retained as incomplete/partial evidence and must not be rewritten as Unavailable merely because the result is incomplete or empty.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceToolProperties` preserves a completed zero-tool result as Available with zero active tools; `VK_INCOMPLETE` with no returned tool entries remains Incomplete and does not prove absence.
- Known `VkResult` values from the pinned Vulkan 1.4.360 header use their canonical registered names while retaining exact numeric values. Unknown future values remain `UNKNOWN(raw=...)`; current registered results must not fall through to the unknown formatter. Image Format Properties2 tuple outcomes retain both raw `VkResult` and canonical result text in structured/UI/TXT/HTML paths.
- Every runtime-enumerated physical device is reachable from the device-specific application pages. A bounded physical-device selector controls Overview, Vulkan, Display/Surface interpretation, Features, Memory, Queues, Formats, Properties, Extensions, Profiles and Analysis instead of silently binding those pages to `devices.firstOrNull()`.
- Multi-device local report filenames do not imply that a complete all-device report contains only the first GPU. Structured/TXT/HTML report content continues to retain every enumerated physical device.
- TXT and HTML summary/hero surfaces must not present the first physical device as though it represents an all-device report. A one-device report may show that device name; a multi-device report uses an explicit physical-device-count summary while detailed sections retain every device.
- HTML HDR summary uses the same state-aware formatter as UI/TXT so a completed empty HDR-type query is never changed back to Unavailable by a presentation-only code path.
- Device-layer absence is displayed only after completed device-layer enumeration proves an empty set. Incomplete, unavailable and unknown device-layer queries retain those states instead of being rendered as `No device layers are exposed`.
- Turnip eligibility remains tri-state in the Vulkan UI. Unknown evidence must not be merged with a completed ineligible result.
- Surface presentation UI maps Available, Incomplete, Not applicable, Unknown and Unavailable independently. An incomplete Surface query is never shown as presentation unsupported.
- Turnip/custom-driver ZIP import rejects duplicate canonical archive paths in addition to path traversal, entry-count, per-file and aggregate-size violations. Canonically equivalent archive entries must not overwrite one another during validation/extraction.
- The isolated Vulkan probe service uses the cache-confined canonical result path after validation for native crash-guard publication and atomic result writing rather than returning to the unnormalized input path.
- Existing complete-report gating, partial `VK_INCOMPLETE` preservation, stale-generation suppression, cross-process identity ambiguity handling, external-capability independence, WSI valid usage, privacy, no-redirect Database submission, update validation, JNI/native ownership, query bounds, three-ABI support, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.23 Vulkan Video routing, partial-evidence and update-version integrity requirements
- Application version is 0.41.23 with versionCode 433. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- The `videoCapabilities` descriptor is an advanced query and its implementation executes only inside `collectVulkanAdvancedGroup`. A duplicate, unreachable or special-cased implementation in the device-extension collector is prohibited.
- Vulkan Video capability probes describe the exact bounded sampled profile recipe being queried. Profile-specific Vulkan Video errors are Unsupported for that sampled profile only; they must not be promoted into codec-wide, device-wide or implementation-wide unsupported claims. Other query failures remain Unavailable with exact `VkResult` evidence.
- Absence of `VK_KHR_video_queue` or a required codec extension is Not applicable only when the authoritative device-extension enumeration completed successfully. Incomplete or unavailable extension enumeration cannot prove absence and retains Unknown evidence.
- Vulkan Video format-query entry-point absence is explicit Unavailable evidence. A successful zero-result format query is valid completed evidence, while `VK_INCOMPLETE` retains bounded partial format rows and explicit incomplete status.
- Vulkan Video capability and format entry points are independent evidence paths. An unavailable `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoCapabilitiesKHR` must not suppress otherwise callable `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoFormatPropertiesKHR` queries, and an unavailable format entry point must not discard sampled-profile capability evidence.
- Encode capability queries obey `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoCapabilitiesKHR` valid usage by chaining both `VkVideoEncodeCapabilitiesKHR` and the codec-specific `VkVideoEncodeH264CapabilitiesKHR`, `VkVideoEncodeH265CapabilitiesKHR` or `VkVideoEncodeAV1CapabilitiesKHR` structure for the sampled encode profile. Codec-specific limits are reported only after `VK_SUCCESS`.
- Vulkan Video format enumeration uses the video usage category being measured rather than generic sampled-image usage: decode output uses `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_VIDEO_DECODE_DST_BIT_KHR`, decode reference/DPB uses `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_VIDEO_DECODE_DPB_BIT_KHR`, encode input uses `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_VIDEO_ENCODE_SRC_BIT_KHR`, and encode reference/DPB uses `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_VIDEO_ENCODE_DPB_BIT_KHR`. Decode and encode categories are retained independently for the bounded sampled profiles.
- Queue-family Vulkan Video operation masks returned while physical-device enumeration is incomplete remain visible as partial positive evidence in UI, Analysis, TXT, HTML and technicalReport together with an explicit Incomplete query state. Unknown, Unavailable and Not applicable states must not fabricate a numeric zero mask.
- The updater validates that the downloaded APK `versionName` exactly matches the normalized version of the selected GitHub release in addition to package identity, signer compatibility, monotonic versionCode, newer-version checks, ABI selection, HTTPS provenance and bounded private-cache download validation. A mismatched release asset is rejected and removed.
- Existing complete-report gating, stale-generation suppression, WSI valid usage, multi-device evidence attribution, external-capability independence, Turnip path confinement, Database no-redirect/all-or-nothing behavior, JNI/native ownership, query bounds, three-ABI support, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory. No Database schema, report-hash or D1 migration is introduced.

## Release 0.41.24 Surface enumeration, bounded partial-evidence and native-lifecycle requirements
- Application version is 0.41.24 with versionCode 434. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport schema remains 3.
- Application-side bounded-allocation rejection is not a Vulkan driver result. Local physical-device safety rejection must be reported through explicit safety/reason provenance and must never fabricate `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY` or any other `VkResult`. Any real returned `VkResult` remains exact and separate.
- Bounded positive evidence returned during `VK_INCOMPLETE` enumeration survives a later retry failure or local safety rejection for physical devices, instance extensions/layers, instance-layer extensions, device layers, device-layer extensions, device extensions and Surface format/present-mode enumeration. Retained evidence is explicitly Incomplete and never proves global absence.
- Physical-device safety rejection accompanying retained partial device handles remains visible in base/Surface report provenance. The base report remains incomplete and TXT/HTML complete export plus Database submission remain disabled until authoritative physical-device enumeration completes.
- Surface format/present-mode retry provenance reflects the last authoritative attempted Vulkan call rather than a stale first-attempt result. An optional formats2/capabilities2 failure falls back to usable classic `VK_KHR_surface` queries without discarding earlier bounded partial positive evidence.
- For a Surface with proven presentation support, a successful Surface-format enumeration containing zero formats and a successful present-mode enumeration containing zero modes or omitting `VK_PRESENT_MODE_FIFO_KHR` are explicit specification anomalies. They are never treated as complete negative capability evidence and cannot authorize unsupported inference for absent catalog entries.
- Surface format/present-mode enumeration completeness and specification-anomaly state flow through DeviceReport, UI, Analysis, TXT, HTML and schema-3 technicalReport. UI catalog completion logic uses those explicit states rather than inferring completeness from one successful `VkResult`.
- The isolated native crash guard restores every successfully replaced signal action after a normal probe finishes and closes its crash-result descriptors deterministically. Crash handling remains async-signal-safe and confined to the isolated probe process.
- Schema-2 legacy single-GPU compatibility summary fields remain backward compatible. When a complete report contains multiple physical devices, additive summary-scope/count provenance makes clear that those legacy fields describe the first physical device while schema-3 technicalReport remains authoritative for all devices.
- Existing Vulkan Video, WSI valid usage, complete-report gating, stale-generation suppression, cross-process identity ambiguity handling, external-capability independence, Turnip path confinement, updater package/version validation, Database HTTPS/no-redirect/all-or-nothing behavior, JNI/native ownership, query bounds, three-ABI support, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.25 Android 17 native dynamic-code hardening requirements
- Application version is 0.41.25 with versionCode 435. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- For 0.41.25, the active Material 3 Expressive pin is `androidx.compose.material3:material3:1.5.0-alpha27`, published 2026-08-26; this supersedes earlier release-specific alpha26 pins for the current tree. Compose UI, Foundation and Animation remain on stable 1.12.0.
- For 0.41.25, the Compose compiler Gradle plugin is pinned to Kotlin `2.4.10`, the current stable Kotlin release. AGP 9 built-in Kotlin remains enabled; `org.jetbrains.kotlin.android` remains prohibited.
- For 0.41.25, OkHttp is pinned to current stable `5.5.0`. The update must preserve VulkanScope's existing explicit system-DNS behavior and approved endpoint set; ECH, DNS-over-HTTPS, AndroidDns or any new network endpoint must not be enabled implicitly by the dependency upgrade. ZXing Core remains 3.5.4.
- User-selected Turnip/AdrenoTools packages remain untrusted input and retain all canonical path, duplicate path, metadata, private-storage, entry-count, per-file, aggregate-size and exact declared-library validation from prior releases.
- Every extracted native `.so` member must be changed to read-only immediately after its output file is opened and before any package bytes are written to that file. Failure to establish read-only state rejects the package before installation.
- Turnip ZIP extraction, expansion bounds, metadata parsing, native-library verification, fsync of security-critical native/metadata files and atomic directory replacement must execute on `Dispatchers.IO`, never on the main/UI thread. The import loop must observe coroutine cancellation and clean its temporary directory on cancellation/failure.
- Only one Turnip import may be in flight per activity. A second picker/import request while extraction is running is rejected before any competing filesystem mutation begins.
- Before the imported bundle is atomically installed, all bounded native `.so` members must be verified readable, canonically confined to the temporary private bundle root and non-writable.
- Every installed Turnip bundle, including legacy bundles, must pass bounded whole-bundle native `.so` read-only verification/hardening before the selected library can be returned to the loader. This applies even when the selected library is already read-only so writable dependent native libraries cannot escape the gate. If hardening fails or any native library remains writable, Turnip resolution fails closed.
- Read-only hardening is additive defense for Android 17 native dynamic-code loading and tamper/race resistance. It does not weaken user initiation, package validation, private-storage confinement, driver selection, isolated-probe execution or native loader ownership rules.
- The change must not alter Vulkan capability evidence, enum/extension naming, query completeness semantics, Surface/WSI behavior, multi-device attribution, TXT/HTML/Database report parity, schema versions, update security, ABI coverage or Vulkan 1.4.360 registry coverage.
- Existing complete-report gating, stale-generation suppression, Database HTTPS/no-redirect/all-or-nothing behavior, updater package/signature/version validation, JNI/native ownership, bounded enumeration, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.26 export-snapshot, driver-mutation and UI-thread correctness requirements
- Application version is 0.41.26 with versionCode 436. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- TXT and HTML complete-report export serialization must not run on the Compose/main thread. The exact report/display/driver snapshot selected at export initiation is serialized on `Dispatchers.IO` into a private cache snapshot before SAF or Downloads destination handling begins.
- A pending SAF export must retain only small saveable metadata identifying the already-persisted private snapshot. Activity/configuration recreation while the document picker is open must not regenerate the report from a newer collection and must not silently lose the intended snapshot merely because Compose state was recreated.
- Report export destination writes use bounded stream copying from the private snapshot rather than an additional full-report UTF-8 byte-array copy. Temporary snapshots are removed after success, cancellation or handled failure, and stale cache snapshots receive bounded cleanup. Failed MediaStore, legacy Downloads and SAF writes must not intentionally leave a known partial report artifact when the destination can be removed.
- Only one complete-report export may be prepared or pending at a time. The complete-report gate remains authoritative at export initiation and TXT/HTML continue to use identical completeness eligibility.
- Installed Turnip whole-bundle validation/hardening introduced in 0.41.25 remains mandatory, but filesystem traversal, canonicalization and permission hardening must execute on `Dispatchers.IO`. Compose recomposition, driver-option click handling and service-intent construction must not perform that blocking bundle walk on the main thread.
- Driver-package selection and driver-source mutation require the complete-report mutation gate both in UI-facing entry points and again in Activity-result callbacks. A picker result that returns after a new Vulkan collection has started must not mutate the active driver package or report inputs.
- Driver-package picker launch has an explicit re-entry guard separate from extraction/import state. Concurrent picker or import activity is rejected before another driver mutation workflow begins.
- While a driver import is mutating private bundle state, new full, Surface and ad-hoc Vulkan collection entry points must not race that mutation. Deferred full/current-state recollection is required when needed after the import finishes or fails.
- A successfully imported Turnip replacement always invalidates the previous report and forces a complete fresh Vulkan collection with the imported driver, including when the selected mode was already Turnip. A new bundle must never be paired with capabilities collected from the previous bundle.
- R8 release optimization must not keep the entire `MainActivity` solely for JNI. The exact `VulkanProbeService` class/native member names required by static JNI entry points remain explicitly protected while unrelated UI code remains optimizable.
- These corrections do not change Vulkan enum/extension names, capability evidence semantics, Surface/WSI valid usage, schema versions, Database payload fields, update endpoints, ABI coverage, Vulkan 1.4.360 registry coverage or the 0.41.25 Android 17 native-code read-only requirement.
- Existing canonical naming, unknown/raw evidence, `VK_INCOMPLETE` retention, complete-report parity, stale-generation suppression, update/Database security, JNI/native ownership, bounded allocation, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.



## Release 0.41.27 checkpoint, incomplete-enumeration and bounded-driver-scan correctness requirements
- Application version is 0.41.27 with versionCode 437. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- Isolated-probe checkpoint polling must not periodically re-read or parse an unchanged result file. File length, modification time and atomic-replacement inode identity gate parsing; an unchanged potentially large checkpoint waits without a repeated JSON allocation/parse pass.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceToolProperties` returning `VK_INCOMPLETE` in either count or data enumeration makes the advanced Tool Properties group Incomplete. Returned tool rows remain bounded positive evidence; zero returned rows do not prove absence.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoFormatPropertiesKHR` returning `VK_INCOMPLETE` for any bounded sampled profile/usage makes the Vulkan Video advanced group Incomplete while retaining all returned format rows as partial positive evidence.
- A device-extension query group must not remain Available when extension enumeration is incomplete or unavailable for any physical device whose extension presence/absence therefore cannot be established. Positive extension evidence from another device remains retained under an explicit Incomplete group status.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceCooperativeMatrixProperties2EXT` `VK_INCOMPLETE` results are retained as bounded partial property evidence and propagate Incomplete to the owning extension query group.
- Installed and newly extracted Turnip bundles use one bounded whole-tree validation pass. The 2048-entry limit counts every visited file and directory rather than only matching `.so` files; canonical aliases/symlink-like path changes, special entries, duplicate canonical paths and entries escaping the private bundle root fail closed. The same validated file set is reused for metadata resolution, declared-library resolution and native read-only hardening.
- Driver bundle installation is serialized with the isolated-probe mutex. While `driverImportInFlight` is true, every isolated probe path including optional self-tests fails/defer-closes instead of racing bundle rename, legacy hardening or driver-path resolution.
- These corrections do not change Vulkan canonical names, unknown/raw evidence policy, report/database schemas, update endpoints, ABI coverage, Surface/WSI valid usage, Android 17 read-only native-code requirement or complete-report export/submission gates.
- Existing stale-generation suppression, exact `VkResult` evidence, bounded allocation, privacy, updater/Database security, JNI/native ownership, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.28 simple-feature completeness and isolated-crash fast-fail requirements
- Application version is 0.41.28 with versionCode 438. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- Every simple extension-backed feature group must propagate device-extension enumeration uncertainty across every enumerated physical device. If an extension is positively present on one device while another device's extension enumeration is incomplete or unavailable, the group root is Incomplete and the positive device evidence remains retained; it must not remain Available.
- Release verification for simple extension-backed feature groups must inspect the `collectVulkanSimpleFeatureGroup` implementation itself rather than accepting completeness markers that exist only in another extension collector. Direct physical-device-only root-status derivation is forbidden in that scoped collector.
- The isolated native crash marker applies to every probe group. Base collection retains its complete-checkpoint recovery rule; advanced, extension, metadata, Surface and self-test probes fail fast to explicit Unavailable when a native signal marker is observed instead of waiting for the generic timeout.
- Crash fast-fail must still terminate the isolated probe process and delete bounded result/crash files through the existing `finally` lifecycle. A crash marker never fabricates unsupported capability evidence.
- These corrections do not change Vulkan canonical names, raw unknown evidence, Vulkan 1.4.360 query coverage, report/database schemas, ABI coverage, Turnip confinement, export snapshots, update endpoints, Surface valid usage or complete-report gates.
- Existing security, bounded allocation/enumeration, stale-generation suppression, JNI/native ownership, source-comment prohibition, UI-thread rules and package hygiene remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.29 base-completeness, extension-state and Turnip traversal requirements
- Application version is 0.41.29 with versionCode 439. The published/query and pinned compile-header baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `baseReportComplete=true` requires completed physical-device enumeration and `deviceExtensionStatus=available` for every returned physical device. Incomplete or unavailable device-extension enumeration may retain bounded positive extension rows locally but must keep the base report incomplete so unscheduled extensions cannot be mistaken for an exhaustive complete report.
- Kotlin base-coverage acceptance independently requires every device-extension enumeration to be Available. A native marker alone cannot authorize TXT/HTML complete export or Database submission when per-device extension provenance is incomplete or unavailable.
- When no device positively enumerates a requested extension, device-extension `VK_INCOMPLETE` remains Incomplete and does not become Unavailable or Not applicable. Not applicable requires complete extension enumeration proving absence; a true enumeration failure with no usable applicability evidence remains Unavailable.
- Advanced groups whose payload or `pNext` chains depend on device-extension presence (`queue2`, `format2`, `imageFormat2`, `external`, `sparse`, `memory2`, `videoCapabilities`) propagate any per-device extension-enumeration uncertainty to an Incomplete group root while retaining bounded positive rows. `tools` and `groups` remain independent of that prerequisite.
- Turnip installed-bundle validation is streaming and bounded across all visited entries. Canonical confinement, alias/symlink-like path equality and regular file/directory type are validated before a directory is queued for descent; validation must not materialize the complete tree with `walkTopDown().toList()`.
- Private Turnip import and backup transaction directories use collision-resistant UUID names. Import requires creation of a new private temporary directory and must never reuse a pre-existing stale transaction directory.
- Existing Vulkan canonical-name/raw-value rules, `VK_INCOMPLETE` positive-evidence retention, report snapshot/export lifecycle, stale-generation suppression, driver/probe mutex, Android 17 read-only native-code hardening, Database/update security, JNI/native ownership, query bounds, three-ABI support, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.30 spec-lock, regression-contract and reproducible-quality-gate requirements
- Application version is 0.41.30 with versionCode 440. The published/query baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- Vulkan upstream provenance is immutable per release. `registry/registry_lock.json` pins the authoritative Vulkan-Docs registry commit `090f1b190d60ced4a1d198fd3747d071cc271b1c`, `xml/vk.xml`, Vulkan-Headers tag `v1.4.360`, header commit `0b7f383797fa7be53ae28213e001ae60668ee511` and `VK_HEADER_VERSION 360`. Floating `main`, unpinned downloads or remembered specification details are not release evidence.
- Every release starts from the last accepted source baseline and records its exact SHA-256. Changes are classified before editing. Working production code is not refactored or rewritten without a demonstrated correctness, specification, security, memory, performance, usability or test-gap reason.
- `tests/golden/0.41.29_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract for 0.41.30. It locks unchanged production files, normalized build configuration, validated query groups, physical-device extension coverage, implemented physical-device structures, instance dependency candidates, Database schema and technicalReport schema. An unexplained difference is release-blocking.
- Production runtime changes require a documented failing regression/specification contract before or with the fix. Updating a golden contract solely to make a failing gate pass is prohibited; the audit record must state the intentional semantic change and its authoritative evidence.
- Checked-in registry metadata must be reproducible. `tools/generate_vk_registry.py` consumes the pinned `vk.xml`, pinned canonical `vulkan_core.h`, native registry catalog, Kotlin validated extension set and registry lock. Strict verification regenerates the manifest and requires semantic JSON equality with the checked-in manifest.
- The stale schema-4 registry-generator path is prohibited. Generator schema, native catalog schema, generated manifest schema and coverage snapshot must remain mutually consistent.
- Strict upstream verification derives all non-provisional Vulkan physical-device Features2/Properties2 extension providers present in the pinned canonical header from `vk.xml`. That derived extension set must equal `VALIDATED_PHYSICAL_DEVICE_QUERY_EXTENSIONS`; no missing extension may be hidden by a hand-maintained count.
- Every runtime pNext structure used by validated physical-device extension collection must exist in the pinned header and registry. Every queried structure type must have report-serialization coverage in the generated/parity field serializers. Query and report coverage are separate gates and both are mandatory.
- Native query descriptors must be unique, must describe exactly the validated runtime query-group set and every device-extension descriptor must be contained in validated physical-device extension coverage. Kotlin validated extension coverage must equal the union of generated and parity pNext extension selectors.
- `tools/verify_regression_contracts.py` is a mandatory local release gate. `tools/quality_gate.py --strict-upstream` is the mandatory upstream/spec gate when locked upstream inputs are available; `--fetch-locked-upstream` may fetch only the exact commits recorded by the lock. A local-only PASS must never be described as a strict upstream PASS.
- `.github/workflows/vulkanscope-quality.yml` runs the locked upstream/spec gate and an Android release build/lint/unit-test gate. A source release must not knowingly bypass a failing CI gate.
- Android/CMake build, lint, unit tests, real-device Vulkan Validation Layers and sanitizer/profiler testing remain distinct evidence classes. Static source review is not a substitute for any of them and no unexecuted class may be reported as PASS.
- Existing 0.41.29 base completeness, `VK_INCOMPLETE` state preservation, advanced extension-dependency semantics, Turnip traversal/transaction safety, canonical Vulkan names/raw unknown values, complete-report export/Database gates, update security, JNI/native ownership, ABI policy, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain byte-for-byte unchanged in the 0.41.30 production runtime baseline.

## Release 0.41.31 spec-correctness, provisional-registry and allowlisted-regression requirements
- Application version is 0.41.31 with versionCode 441. The authoritative published/query baseline remains Vulkan 1.4.360 dated 2026-08-14 at Vulkan-Docs commit `090f1b190d60ced4a1d198fd3747d071cc271b1c`; Vulkan-Headers remains commit `0b7f383797fa7be53ae28213e001ae60668ee511`, header version 360. Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.30_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract for 0.41.31. Every predecessor runtime file remains byte-identical unless that exact path is allowlisted with successor SHA-256, defect category, evidence and a regression test. For this release only `app/src/main/cpp/vulkanscope.cpp` may change.
- `VK_FORMAT_A1B5G5R5_UNORM_PACK16` and `VK_FORMAT_A8_UNORM` format-property queries are eligible when provided by core Vulkan 1.4 or by `VK_KHR_maintenance5`; extension-provided Vulkan 1.3 capability must not be silently omitted.
- `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TENSOR_ALIASING_ARM` is a canonical Vulkan image-layout name and must be reported by that exact identifier when its numeric value is observed. Genuinely unknown future values retain explicit raw numeric evidence and must never receive an invented `VK_*` name.
- WSI color-space reporting must preserve specification semantics rather than infer end-to-end media capability. `VK_COLOR_SPACE_DCI_P3_NONLINEAR_EXT` records that presentation interprets components as XYZ. `VK_COLOR_SPACE_DOLBYVISION_EXT` is identified as the legacy Vulkan color-space enum and must not by itself be presented as proof of active Dolby Vision metadata signaling.
- Strict locked-registry coverage includes every registered Vulkan physical-device Features2/Properties2 extension provider that is representable by the pinned header, including registered provisional providers. Stable and provisional providers are counted and recorded separately. A provisional runtime query requires explicit `VK_ENABLE_BETA_EXTENSIONS` header mode; provisional status must never be hidden or conflated with stable status. This requirement supersedes the 0.41.30 wording that excluded provisional providers from the strict exact-match set.
- The checked-in extension-reference detail asset may only claim metadata actually derived from its pinned source. Missing embedded reference detail remains explicitly unavailable; vendor, revision, type, platform, promotion or dependency metadata must never be guessed to fill an incomplete asset. Full reference regeneration from locked `vk.xml` is required before claiming that embedded reference metadata is complete.
- `tools/verify_spec_regressions.py`, the golden predecessor contract, release verifier and combined quality gate are mandatory release gates. A targeted spec correction must fail against the predecessor and pass against the successor before its production change is accepted.
- Complete-report gating, base/device-extension completeness, `VK_INCOMPLETE` retention, Surface/WSI evidence, stale-generation suppression, TXT/HTML/Analysis/Database parity, update package/signature/version validation, fixed Database HTTPS/no-redirect/all-or-nothing behavior, Turnip path and native-code hardening, deterministic Vulkan/JNI/native ownership, bounded allocations, three-ABI policy, main-thread blocking prohibition, source-comment prohibition and package hygiene remain mandatory.
- Static audit, local quality-gate PASS, Android build/lint/unit tests, real-device Vulkan Validation Layers and sanitizer/profiler evidence remain distinct evidence classes. No unexecuted class may be described as PASS.



## Release 0.41.32 Vulkan 1.4.361, registry-census, concurrency and resource-budget requirements
- Application version is 0.41.32 with versionCode 442. The authoritative registry/query baseline is Vulkan 1.4.361 dated 2026-08-28. The current published specification is Vulkan 1.4.361 dated 2026-08-28. The bundled canonical `vk.xml` is SHA-256 locked to `3ff4984b841932e04eebeb4ce2a6613ebd37c00ffb2e96549785b2c5d7da9e1d`; Vulkan-Headers is pinned to tag `v1.4.361`, commit `31386378257ac8653ce5b32c93baec385259ebbe`, header version 361.
- `tests/golden/0.41.31_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract. Every production/build-chain file from 0.41.31 remains byte-identical unless its exact path is allowlisted with successor SHA-256, defect category, evidence and regression-test provenance.
- The 1.4.361 registry delta is derived from the locked `vk.xml`, not from remembered extension lists. `VK_NV_private_data_base_handle` and `VkPhysicalDevicePrivateDataBaseHandleFeaturesNV::privateDataBaseHandle` are queried and serialized when the runtime enumerates the extension.
- The registry reference asset is regenerated from the locked `vk.xml` and must represent the complete registered Vulkan extension census for that registry snapshot. Missing runtime support is never inferred from registry registration, and runtime-enumerated extension names/specVersion remain authoritative device evidence.
- Strict physical-device provider coverage resolves Vulkan type aliases to canonical registry types before comparing pNext scheduling. Alias-equivalent EXT/KHR/core structures are one semantic provider and must not create false missing-query failures.
- Stable and provisional Features2/Properties2 providers are tracked separately. Provisional queries require `VK_ENABLE_BETA_EXTENSIONS`; provisional status remains visible in generated metadata and must never be silently treated as stable.
- The service process is a dedicated `:vulkan_probe` application process, not Android `isolatedProcess=true`; user-facing/report terminology must not overclaim OS process isolation. Probe calls are serialized by the application mutex, a single service worker and the fair native probe lock. Timeout measurement begins after the serialized probe launch owns its slot, and timeout/crash paths terminate only the app-owned `:vulkan_probe` process after UID/process-name validation.
- Native probe success data is published directly to an atomic bounded checkpoint file. JNI must return only publication success/failure and must not duplicate a potentially large complete JSON report through `NewStringUTF`. Main-process checkpoint reading uses one exact-size byte buffer from the opened inode and rejects size/short-read/growth anomalies.
- The per-probe publication ceiling is 64 MiB and is fail-closed without truncation. The 2 MiB Database transport ceiling and 8 MiB Analysis snapshot ceiling are separate downstream limits and must not be described as proof that every theoretically possible complete report fits. Any oversize result is explicit and must never be promoted to a complete report.
- Resource-budget verification records the canonical registered extension census and confirms native extension enumeration capacity remains above it. This is a safety-headroom check, not a mathematical proof of a maximum possible report size.
- The spec-driven + regression-gated workflow is mandatory for all future VulkanScope changes: immutable predecessor baseline, current canonical upstream pin, machine-generated coverage, failing-before-fix regression evidence, allowlisted production diffs, negative gate tests, build/lint/test evidence where executable, and explicit disclosure of any verification class that could not run. Working production code is not changed without a proven specification, correctness, security, memory, performance, usability or test-gap reason.

## Release 0.41.33 Vulkan 1.4.361 CMake registry-lock build-regression requirements
- Application version is 0.41.33 with versionCode 443; Vulkan API/registry baseline remains 1.4.361, schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- The canonical bundled Vulkan-Docs `registry/upstream/vk.xml` snapshot remains byte-for-byte SHA-256 locked to `3ff4984b841932e04eebeb4ce2a6613ebd37c00ffb2e96549785b2c5d7da9e1d`.
- Vulkan-Headers remains pinned to exact commit `31386378257ac8653ce5b32c93baec385259ebbe` / tag `v1.4.361` and must expose `VK_HEADER_VERSION 361`.
- CMake must not require the Vulkan-Headers repository copy of `registry/vk.xml` to have the Vulkan-Docs snapshot byte hash. Cross-repository registry validation is semantic: header version 361 plus the Vulkan 1.4.361 sentinel extension must be present.
- A build-regression verifier must fail if the fetched Vulkan-Headers registry is again byte-hashed against the Vulkan-Docs SHA, if the bundled canonical snapshot SHA gate is removed, or if exact header commit/version checks drift.
- No runtime query/report/database schema behavior is changed by this release.


## Release 0.41.34 real release-compiler regression requirements
- Application version is 0.41.34 with versionCode 444. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361; Database submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.33_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract. Only `app/src/main/cpp/vulkanscope.cpp` and `app/src/main/java/com/efishell/vulkanscope/MainActivity.kt` may change in production for this release, with exact successor hashes and supplied compiler evidence.
- Real compiler failures outrank a previous static quality-gate PASS. Any build-stopping undeclared identifier, unresolved Kotlin helper or `-Werror` dead symbol must receive a failing-before-fix regression guard before release.
- Base-collector WSI/dependency booleans may use bounded instance-extension enumeration as positive evidence, but incomplete enumeration must never be converted into definite extension absence.
- The extension-group collector must not retain unused local helpers under the project `-Werror` policy.
- HTML Device layer enumeration must use the existing `table(...)` helper; an undeclared `kv(...)` helper is prohibited.
