## Release 0.41.35 Turnip post-checkpoint lifecycle and bounded-detail-collection requirements
- Application version is 0.41.35 with versionCode 445. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361; Database submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.34_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract. Production changes are limited to `vulkanscope.cpp`, `MainActivity.kt` and `VulkanProbeService.kt` for the demonstrated post-checkpoint lifecycle defect.
- An atomically published `baseReportComplete=true` checkpoint is authoritative evidence and must not be discarded merely because custom-driver cleanup, service shutdown or timeout processing stalls afterward.
- Turnip runs in the dedicated `:vulkan_probe` process. Potentially unbounded custom-driver `vkDestroyInstance`, `vkDestroySurfaceKHR` and loader-handle teardown must not run on the successful result-publication critical path; their resources are reclaimed by one-shot process termination. System-driver cleanup semantics remain unchanged.
- After the main process accepts any valid atomic probe publication, it must terminate the previous dedicated probe process before the next serialized query can use that slot. A timeout must recover an already-published valid terminal result before fabricating an Unavailable timeout result.
- Automatic detailed collection remains exhaustive in scheduling, but has a 60-second total background budget in addition to existing per-query timeouts. Any groups not attempted because that total budget expires must receive explicit bounded Unavailable evidence through the normal merge path and must have their collection tasks completed; silent omission and indefinitely active collection status are prohibited.
- `tools/verify_probe_lifecycle_regressions.py` is mandatory in the quality gate and must fail against 0.41.34 while passing against 0.41.35.
- Vulkan 1.4.361 registry lock, 302 validated physical-device extensions, 110 implemented structs, base-report completeness, Surface evidence semantics, 64 MiB publication cap, 2 MiB Database transport cap, 8 MiB Analysis cap, three-ABI policy, security controls and Database 0.39.14 compatibility remain unchanged.
- Static quality-gate PASS, Android release build PASS and repaired real-device Turnip behavior are distinct evidence classes; unexecuted classes must not be claimed as PASS.
## Release 0.41.36 terminal probe-publication handshake and evidence-workflow requirements
- Application version is 0.41.36 with versionCode 446. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; Database schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.35_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract and records the exact accepted 0.41.35 ZIP SHA-256 `c3fdfcf718fddf53cac0dd98f5886436df2f5392d8d9b75e171d03a082f3f28f`. Production changes are limited to `vulkanscope.cpp`, `MainActivity.kt` and `VulkanProbeService.kt`.
- The supplied real-device failure signature is mandatory regression evidence: native base collection publishes the complete checkpoint, `VulkanProbeService` self-SIGKILLs immediately, the main process fails to accept that publication, waits for the base timeout and retries. The predecessor must fail the new handshake regression before the successor is accepted.
- A dedicated probe process must never self-SIGKILL before the producer-to-consumer terminal handoff is observable. The service publishes an atomic terminal marker only after the final bounded result publication returns; process termination after result acceptance remains owned by the main process.
- A terminal marker forces a fresh bounded result-file reread even when inode, mtime or length are unchanged from a previously observed checkpoint. A marker paired with missing, oversized, malformed or non-terminal JSON fails closed immediately rather than waiting for the normal probe timeout.
- Base terminal JSON contains one canonical `baseReportComplete` key. Intermediate base-ready checkpoints must be structurally closed JSON and cannot be logged as published unless atomic checkpoint publication succeeded.
- `tools/test_probe_publication_state_machine.py` is the behavioral/state-machine contract. It covers final publication after an earlier invalid/intermediate observation, terminal forced reread, malformed-terminal fast-fail and an unrelated-marker false-positive control. `tools/verify_probe_publication_handshake.py` binds that behavioral protocol to the production implementation.
- Negative mutation evidence must fail when service self-SIGKILL is restored, terminal forced reread is removed, canonical terminal JSON construction is broken or an unallowlisted production file changes. The unrelated-file/marker false-positive control must remain PASS.
- The complete quality gate, package hygiene gate and state-machine contract are rerun after the release ZIP is extracted. Source and extracted package files must be byte-identical.
- Android `assembleRelease`, lint/unit tests and real-device reproduction of the original Turnip failure remain separate evidence classes. They may be reported PASS only when actually executed; otherwise they are explicitly `NOT EXECUTED`.
- Existing Vulkan 1.4.361 registry census, 302 validated physical-device extensions, 110 implemented structs, complete-report/export/Database gates, 60-second bounded background-detail collection, security/privacy controls, 64 MiB probe publication cap, 2 MiB Database transport cap, 8 MiB Analysis cap and three-ABI policy remain unchanged.

## Release 0.41.37 pre-return terminal publication, one-shot process isolation and bounded base-timeout requirements
- Application version is 0.41.37 with versionCode 447. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; Database schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.36_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract and records the exact accepted 0.41.36 ZIP SHA-256 `2c3a9a74929349d8de70949b776777308ea4a5e89d8386a594e499851842b52d`. Production/runtime changes are limited to `vulkanscope.cpp`, `MainActivity.kt` and `VulkanProbeService.kt`; resource-budget metadata changes only to reflect the demonstrated base timeout ceiling.
- The 0.41.36 handoff test gap is release-blocking: `.done` was modeled only after service/JNI return even though native base collection can make a terminal JSON durable before JNI/service return. The successor behavioral contract must accept a durable terminal result after producer death even if service return never occurs.
- Native base terminal publication writes the bounded final JSON and its exact `.done` sidecar before any Vulkan/object/loader teardown. A terminal marker may never precede its final result. Service-level marker publication remains an idempotent fallback.
- Because `libvulkanscope.so` is loaded only by the non-exported dedicated `:vulkan_probe` service process, Vulkan instance/surface/loader teardown for probe calls is process-owned for both system and Turnip modes. Driver cleanup may not occupy the result-publication/return critical path; process exit reclaims those resources.
- A one-shot probe service may self-terminate only after durable terminal publication. The main consumer must also enforce a bounded stale-process quiescence barrier before every new probe so leaked driver/instance state cannot be reused by a subsequent query.
- Base probe timeout is 20 seconds. A terminal `unavailable` or timeout result is not automatically repeated. At most one retry is permitted only for an explicit `incomplete` result retaining partial positive device evidence and not caused by timeout. This prevents the previous deterministic 45s × 2 failure amplification.
- Hard timeout termination must not depend solely on `ActivityManager.runningAppProcesses`. The dedicated service process must arm its own bounded hard-deadline watchdog from the exact consumer timeout, and the consumer timeout path must settle beyond that watchdog before allowing the next serialized request. Pre-terminal process self-exit is permitted only for that hard deadline or an explicit consumer/service cancellation teardown.
- Service destruction is process-owned cancellation: `stopService`/`onDestroy` must terminate the dedicated `:vulkan_probe` process even when the executor is blocked inside JNI. `shutdownNow()` or coroutine cancellation alone is insufficient because neither can interrupt arbitrary native driver code.
- `runServiceProbe` cancellation must enter a non-cancellable teardown path, request service/process shutdown, allow a bounded stop acknowledgement/settle window independent of ActivityManager visibility, and only then remove request-scoped result/marker files and rethrow `CancellationException`. A cancelled request must never be converted into Vulkan capability evidence.
- `tools/test_probe_timeout_state_machine.py`, `tools/verify_probe_timeout_recovery.py`, `tools/test_probe_cancellation_state_machine.py` and `tools/verify_probe_cancellation_recovery.py` are mandatory. The recovery verifiers must fail against immutable 0.41.36 and pass against 0.41.37. Negative mutations must fail if pre-return terminal publication, process-owned teardown, post-terminal self-exit, stale-process barrier, timeout non-retry or cancellation teardown semantics are removed; unrelated terminal files/processes remain false-positive controls.
- Existing terminal forced-reread, canonical single-key `baseReportComplete`, crash/oversize fail-closed behavior, 60-second exhaustive background-detail budget, Vulkan 1.4.361 census/coverage, 64 MiB probe cap, 2 MiB Database transport cap, 8 MiB Analysis cap, security/privacy controls and Database 0.39.14 compatibility remain unchanged.
- Static/spec gates, real Android build/lint/unit, sanitizer/profiler evidence and real-device reproduction remain separate evidence classes. Device runtime may be called fixed only after the original timeout scenario is rerun successfully.


## Release 0.41.38 single terminal-owner and post-JNI handoff requirements
- Application version is 0.41.38 with versionCode 448. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; Database schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.37_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract and records the exact accepted 0.41.37 ZIP SHA-256 `99ca00817dee7d0f2a0c5ab6b99df02e56072fa2bd87876dcdb5af4586404052`.
- Real-device evidence supersedes the 0.41.37 pre-return-terminal-marker clause: a result/checkpoint made durable before JNI return is provisional evidence, not a normal terminal handoff. Native code must never publish the request `.done` sidecar. Only `VulkanProbeService` may publish `.done`, and only after the JNI call has returned, the final result has no remaining writer, and bounded streaming validation establishes terminal JSON.
- Base JNI must not rewrite an already durable final checkpoint produced by the native collector. If the collector did not publish its final result, JNI may perform one bounded fallback publication after collection returns.
- Normal consumer polling must never promote a terminal-shaped result/checkpoint without the exact request `.done` marker. Such a pre-marker result may be retained only for crash/timeout boundary recovery after producer termination/settle. An unrelated marker never changes state.
- Marker observation requires a bounded stable-result reread window before terminal acceptance. Missing, oversized, malformed or non-terminal payload after that window fails closed; it must not consume an older checkpoint.
- Hard deadline, normal completion and explicit cancellation/service destruction must share one atomic process-termination claim. Production service code has one `Process.killProcess(Process.myPid())` call site behind that claim; duplicate worker/onDestroy SIGKILL races are forbidden.
- Service-side terminal validation is bounded by the existing 64 MiB result ceiling and uses streaming JSON parsing. If JNI returns after publishing invalid terminal JSON, the service replaces it with explicit bounded `unavailable` evidence before publishing `.done`; malformed data is never blessed as a successful terminal handoff.
- `tools/test_probe_terminal_ownership_state_machine.py` and `tools/verify_probe_terminal_ownership.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.37 and pass against 0.41.38. Negative mutations must fail if native `.done` publication is restored, pre-marker normal acceptance is restored, the atomic termination claim is bypassed, service post-JNI validation is removed, or an unallowlisted production file changes.
- Existing 20-second base timeout, hard watchdog, cancellation `NonCancellable` teardown, stale-process barrier, timeout recovery, 60-second background-detail budget, Vulkan 1.4.361 coverage, resource/security limits and Database 0.39.14 compatibility remain unchanged.

## Release 0.41.39 base terminal JSON structural-integrity requirements
- Application version is 0.41.39 with versionCode 449. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; Database schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.38_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract and records the exact accepted 0.41.38 ZIP SHA-256 `0d537fe2f4a3488503f1278eaf13e3c71eed1521db05ffac66efdba6b8a8e4f0`.
- The supplied 0.41.38 real-device log is the mandatory failure oracle: native base collection reaches `base report complete checkpoint published before JNI return; terminal marker remains service-owned`, then the service immediately reports `Native probe returned after publishing malformed or non-terminal JSON`. A successor may not be released unless the predecessor fails and the successor passes the base-terminal JSON regression contract.
- Every base-report device object must be structurally closed before the `devices` array is closed. This requirement applies to one-device and multi-device reports; a syntactically terminal-looking prefix is not sufficient evidence.
- Before a base result is published/logged as the final checkpoint, native code must reject unbalanced JSON object/array containers while respecting quoted strings and escapes. The service streaming parser remains the authoritative post-JNI terminal validation and is not removed or weakened.
- The one-shot base collector must not execute `vkDestroyInstance` on its return/publication critical path. Base Vulkan instance lifetime is process-owned in the dedicated probe process; hard timeout and service termination reclaim it through process death as required by the existing 0.41.37/0.41.38 lifecycle rules.
- `tools/test_base_terminal_json_state_machine.py` and `tools/verify_base_terminal_json.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.38 and pass against 0.41.39. Negative mutations must fail if the per-device close is removed, structural native validation is bypassed, base `vkDestroyInstance` is restored, or an unallowlisted production path changes; the multi-device valid fixture is the false-positive control.
- Existing service-owned post-JNI `.done` ownership, single atomic process termination claim, 20-second base timeout, cancellation `NonCancellable` teardown, stale-process barrier, 60-second background-detail budget, Vulkan 1.4.361 coverage, security/resource limits and Database 0.39.14 compatibility remain unchanged.

## Release 0.41.40 report-field semantics, evidence-state and Database-contract requirements
- Application version is 0.41.40 with versionCode 450. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.39_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract and records the exact accepted 0.41.39 ZIP SHA-256 `11e94bad2605e87c1e3a484a2905843ac6a1495180d2855c6a0a7e62b8ff26d6`.
- Physical-device field classification is derived from the canonical Vulkan struct/member role, never from C++ typedef identity. `VkBool32` is typedef-compatible with `uint32_t`; therefore `std::is_same` or an equivalent underlying-C++-type test must not decide whether a queried member is a feature.
- A `VkBool32` member of a `VkPhysicalDevice*Features*` struct remains boolean feature evidence. A `VkBool32` member of a `VkPhysicalDevice*Properties*` struct remains a detailed property with literal `true`/`false` value evidence. Numeric property zero is a queried numeric value and must never be rewritten as `NOT SUPPORTED` merely because its underlying type is integer-compatible with `VkBool32`.
- Registry/parity generated coverage must route every canonical `VkBool32` member through the explicit boolean emitter before C++ compilation. Generic auto emission is property-only; a feature field routed through the auto emitter is a release failure. Struct aliases are resolved through the locked 1.4.361 `vk.xml` before this contract is evaluated.
- Structured Vulkan values with an implemented formatter remain structured in detailed reports. `VkExtent2D` and `VkExtent3D` are rendered as dimensions, and `VkConformanceVersion` is rendered as `major.minor.subminor.patch`; they are not intentionally degraded to opaque raw bytes. Exact raw bytes remain valid evidence only for types without a canonical formatter in the current collector.
- HTML status color is semantic and supplemental to explicit text. `Available`, `Supported`, `True`, `Yes` and `Pass` are positive; `Unsupported`, `Not supported`, `False`, `No`, `Fail`, `Error`, `Not available` and `Not exposed` are negative; `Unavailable` is a distinct query/evidence failure state; `Not applicable` is distinct neutral applicability evidence; `Incomplete` is distinct partial evidence; `Unknown`/`Not reported` remain unknown. `Unavailable`, `Unsupported`, `Not applicable`, `Incomplete` and `Unknown` must never be collapsed into one visual or semantic state.
- Blank successful query reasons in HTML are rendered explicitly as `None` rather than an empty cell. This presentation rule does not fabricate a reason or convert missing query evidence into availability.
- Detailed-query totals are evidence counts, not Vulkan-property-count claims. Total evidence rows equal property/query rows plus the four retained `Vulkan Query Safety` diagnostics when those diagnostics are present. A release must not pin a cosmetic historical number such as 1477; counts are derived from actual classified evidence and may legitimately change when previously misclassified property rows are restored.
- The 0.41.39 supplied HTML report is a mandatory regression oracle for this release: it demonstrates red `AVAILABLE` badges and physical-device `Properties` members rendered as `SUPPORTED`/`NOT SUPPORTED`. The successor source verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.39 and pass against 0.41.40.
- `tools/verify_report_semantics_04140.py` and `tools/test_report_semantics_state_machine.py` are mandatory. Negative mutations must fail when Available is made negative, a canonical property/feature field is routed through the wrong emitter, opaque conformance-version serialization is restored, or an unallowlisted production file changes. A feature bool and a property bool are explicit false-positive controls.
- The companion Database must be updated to 0.39.15 because current producer TXT report identity separates `Loader API` from `Base probe instance API`; Database validation must preserve historical merged-line compatibility while accepting and strictly validating the current split form. No application submission-schema, technicalReport-schema, endpoint, hash algorithm or D1 migration change is introduced.
- Existing base terminal JSON integrity, service-owned post-JNI handoff, single process-termination ownership, 20-second base timeout, cancellation teardown, 60-second background-detail budget, Vulkan 1.4.361 registry coverage, query-state provenance, privacy/security/resource bounds and package/extract/reverify requirements remain mandatory.

## Release 0.41.41 Surface identity, binary-property and provenance requirements
- Application version is 0.41.41 with versionCode 451. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- `tests/golden/0.41.40_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract and records VulkanScope 0.41.40 ZIP SHA-256 `b85f3df2a78496be08dd8634ac94d0e97732e1e7f32524dd6e8d72ad9475deb2`.
- The supplied 0.41.40 Turnip report comparison is a mandatory regression oracle. It demonstrates `vkCreateAndroidSurfaceKHR` returning `VkResult=-1000000001` followed by loss of previously reported Surface capabilities, presentation queues and format/color-space evidence. A successor must not convert a Surface-only lifecycle event into a second complete report collection.
- Android Surface state is identity- and generation-bound. A destroyed/stale Surface callback must never clear a newer live Surface. A delayed result for an older Surface generation must never overwrite the current report; it may only schedule one refresh for the current live Surface.
- Surface refresh and complete report collection are separate state transitions. Surface creation/change during active collection is deferred as a Surface refresh, not promoted to `collectionPending` full recollection. Current driver mutation remains allowed to request full recollection.
- `VK_ERROR_NATIVE_WINDOW_IN_USE_KHR` from `vkCreateAndroidSurfaceKHR` is Unavailable evidence for that attempt, never Unsupported. The Surface probe may perform one bounded delayed retry while the same Surface remains live. A second failure remains explicit Unavailable evidence and must not loop.
- Fixed-size `uint8_t` Vulkan property arrays are binary evidence. They must be serialized as exact raw bytes with byte count and hexadecimal payload, never streamed as character text. This includes applicable UUID fields such as `optimalTilingLayoutUUID`, `shaderModuleIdentifierAlgorithmUUID` and `shaderBinaryUUID`.
- Generated detailed-property deduplication is section-aware. Same-name members from distinct property structs retain their struct provenance. An equivalent manually collected extension property may be upgraded to the canonical generated struct section, but unrelated or differently valued evidence is not silently discarded.
- `tools/verify_report_surface_integrity_04141.py`, `tools/test_report_surface_integrity_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_report_surface_integrity_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The source verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.40 and pass against 0.41.41; negative mutations must reject byte-array downgrade, sectionless property dedup and destroyed-Surface identity loss. An unrelated diagnostic-text change is the false-positive control.
- Device runtime reproduction after the fix is a separate evidence class. Static/source/state-machine PASS must never be presented as real-device Surface PASS when no device execution occurred.
- Companion Database is 0.39.16. Application schema, endpoint, report hashing, privacy boundaries, non-truncating transport behavior, terminal handoff ownership and all prior security/resource gates remain unchanged.

## Release 0.41.43 driver-bound Surface rebind requirements
- Application version is 0.41.43 with versionCode 453. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target remains API 37; submission schema remains 2 and technicalReport remains 3.
- Treat VulkanScope 0.41.42 as the immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `8ca0dc49c2e4eb015fe18c293c444a9bc826dc8a768f36b7058eb871ba50ee33`.
- The supplied real-device driver-switch failure is the regression oracle: after changing the selected Vulkan driver source, Surface evidence may become Unavailable until the Android Surface is manually recreated. Missing Surface evidence after driver mutation is never reclassified as Unsupported.
- A real driver-mode change and forced same-mode Turnip package activation must invalidate the pre-change Surface identity and generation before successor collection begins.
- Driver mutation must advance a Surface host generation and recreate the Compose-owned `SurfaceView`; a complete report for the new driver must wait for the replacement host to publish a live Surface rather than probing the previous driver generation's native-window identity.
- Surface created/destroyed callbacks are host-generation bound. Late callbacks from an older host may not become current, clear a newer live Surface or trigger collection for the new driver generation.
- Ordinary Surface destruction/recreation without driver mutation remains Surface-only refresh behavior and must not be promoted to a full report recollection. Duplicate callbacks for the same current Surface remain no-ops.
- The existing one bounded retry for `VK_ERROR_NATIVE_WINDOW_IN_USE_KHR`, stale Surface-result generation checks and explicit Unavailable evidence remain mandatory; driver rebind must not add an unbounded retry or hide a second failure.
- `tools/test_driver_surface_rebind_state_machine.py`, `tools/verify_driver_surface_rebind_04143.py` and `tools/test_driver_surface_rebind_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The source verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.42 and pass against 0.41.43. Negative mutation evidence must reject host recreation removal, stale-host acceptance and driver-change rebind removal, with an unrelated diagnostic-text false-positive control.
- Vulkan query coverage, UUID/raw-byte serialization, property provenance, HTML state presentation, submission payloads, endpoint, schemas, report hashing, privacy/security/resource bounds and all previous lifecycle/publication gates remain unchanged.
- Real-device reproduction of the original driver-switch failure is a separate evidence class and remains `NOT EXECUTED` until the driver is changed on device without a manual Surface toggle and the new Surface probe completes Available.

## Release 0.41.44 authoritative Vulkan Profiles evaluation requirements
- Application version is 0.41.44 with versionCode 454. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target, submission schema 2 and technicalReport schema 3 remain unchanged.
- Treat VulkanScope 0.41.43 as immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `48565d324d216f80f0278814047d19539cb32c05724411b36b55199430611ffa`.
- The demonstrated failure class is incomplete/profile-name-only evaluation: a listed `VP_*` row must not imply that the authoritative profile graph was checked when only API version or a small hand-selected subset was inspected.
- Profile requirements used by 0.41.44 must be tied to `registry/profiles/profile_requirements_lock.json`; no floating network profile definition may be consulted at runtime. Current release source refs are Vulkan-Headers `v1.4.361` for `VP_KHR_roadmap.json` and Vulkan-Profiles `v1.4.361` for the audited Android profile files.
- Feature evidence identity is structure plus member. Same-name members in unrelated structures may not satisfy one another without an explicit audited alias/promotion mapping.
- Extension absence is FAIL only after the owning enumeration completed Available. Missing/incomplete enumeration remains UNKNOWN. Core promotion may satisfy an extension only through checked-in promotion metadata and a sufficient runtime API version.
- Property checks must use explicit directional semantics: minimum, maximum, boolean equality, bitmask containment or vector minimum. A parse failure is UNKNOWN, never a guessed PASS/FAIL.
- Format requirements use queried format feature masks. Missing format evidence is UNKNOWN; a present format missing a required bit is FAIL.
- Required-profile inheritance and OR capability groups are first-class evaluator semantics. Required-profile cycles are UNKNOWN/rejected; OR succeeds when one alternative is fully verified, fails only when all alternatives are verified failures, and otherwise remains UNKNOWN.
- Verified missing requirement yields FAIL even when overall coverage is partial. PASS requires both zero failed/unknown requirements and `completeCoverage=true`. Any unmapped requirement class/profile or coverage-limited normalized source keeps a non-failing result UNKNOWN.
- Android 15/16/17 direct requirement graphs are normalized with their required-profile and OR relationships. Android 15 subgroup operation requirement is exactly mask `0x3F`; `0xBF` is prohibited. Android Vulkan Profile 2025 remains coverage-limited until its complete official property/format matrix is source-equivalent; Android 2022/2021 remain catalog-only UNKNOWN until normalized.
- Roadmap 2022/2024/2026 must perform mapped extension/feature/property/OR checks rather than API-only evaluation. Roadmap 2026 uses its exact official composition and must not inherit Roadmap-2024-only promoted-v1.4 or line-alternative groups. Roadmap PASS remains disabled until independent full source-equivalence is proven.
- LunarG minimum-requirements catalog entries have no API-only support inference while their exact authoritative definitions are not pinned. They remain explicit UNKNOWN.
- Profiles UI, Analysis, JSON snapshot, TXT and HTML consume `vulkanProfileEvaluations()` as the canonical evaluation source; mapped and catalog-only identities must not diverge across surfaces.
- `tools/verify_profile_requirements_04144.py`, `tools/test_profile_evaluator_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_profile_requirements_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The source verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.43 and pass against 0.41.44. Negative mutation coverage must reject mask drift, coverage-gate removal, Roadmap over-inheritance, stale UI source and API-only catalog support, with an unrelated UI-text false-positive control.
- Existing Vulkan query coverage (302 queryable provider extensions / 110 physical-device structs / 104 validated query groups), Surface/driver-generation handling, binary UUID/property serialization, report evidence counts, schemas, endpoint/security/privacy/resource bounds and all previous release contracts remain unchanged unless separately demonstrated and contracted.
- Real-device profile evaluation is a separate evidence class and is NOT EXECUTED unless a device run is actually recorded.

## Release 0.41.45 locked Vulkan Video StdVideo registry census requirements
- Application version is 0.41.45 with versionCode 455. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; Android compile/target, submission schema 2 and technicalReport schema 3 remain unchanged.
- Treat VulkanScope 0.41.44 as immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `01d6953384471b1191d81a6ef59d74257cc1cab133cff2e9aee47a1e766314f7`.
- The demonstrated gap is sampled codec-profile capability coverage: 0.41.44 queried one representative profile per Vulkan Video codec operation even though the canonical registries define additional codec-specific profile-member values. Existing sampled rows were not false codec-wide claims, but full current codec-member census is required for release 0.41.45.
- `registry/upstream/video.xml` is a release-locked Khronos Vulkan-Docs `xml/video.xml` snapshot identified by exact content ref `content-sha256:d018b914014c06605e367a3b929670511e6f6de2f225c405a8b5e2d912408b76` and SHA-256 `d018b914014c06605e367a3b929670511e6f6de2f225c405a8b5e2d912408b76`; a floating branch ref is not release evidence. It is authoritative only for StdVideo structures/enumerations and codec-standard profile/level identities. `registry/upstream/vk.xml` remains the Vulkan 1.4.361 API/profile registry with SHA-256 `3ff4984b841932e04eebeb4ce2a6613ebd37c00ffb2e96549785b2c5d7da9e1d` and is authoritative for Vulkan Video API structures and codec-specific `videoprofiles` member combinations. Neither registry is runtime device-support evidence.
- Runtime must perform zero network access for registry/profile evaluation. `tools/generate_video_registry.py` must verify both locked hashes, cross-check every Vulkan codec profile token against video.xml and deterministically generate `video_registry_generated.h`; stale generated output is a release failure.
- Capability enumeration uses one explicit general-profile slice only: `VK_VIDEO_CHROMA_SUBSAMPLING_420_BIT_KHR`, 8-bit luma and 8-bit chroma. It then exhaustively enumerates the current registry-defined codec-specific members: H.264 decode six profiles times three picture layouts; H.265 decode five profiles; VP9 decode four profiles; AV1 decode three profiles times both film-grain values; H.264 encode six profiles; H.265 encode five profiles; AV1 encode three profiles. The bounded maximum is 47 `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoCapabilitiesKHR` calls when all owning extensions are positively present.
- A successful capability result proves only that exact 4:2:0 8-bit profile combination. No codec-wide, chroma-wide, bit-depth-wide or profile-family support may be inferred from it. General-profile combinations not queried remain unreported rather than guessed.
- `vkGetPhysicalDeviceVideoCapabilitiesKHR` produces Unsupported only for its documented exact profile failure results: `VK_ERROR_VIDEO_PICTURE_LAYOUT_NOT_SUPPORTED_KHR`, `VK_ERROR_VIDEO_PROFILE_OPERATION_NOT_SUPPORTED_KHR`, `VK_ERROR_VIDEO_PROFILE_FORMAT_NOT_SUPPORTED_KHR` and `VK_ERROR_VIDEO_PROFILE_CODEC_NOT_SUPPORTED_KHR`. OOM, validation, unknown or any other non-success result remains Unavailable.
- Required codec/queue extension absence is Not applicable only when device-extension enumeration completed Available. Incomplete/unavailable enumeration preserves Unknown; positive extension evidence may still permit the query.
- Returned H.264/H.265/VP9/AV1 maximum levels use canonical names generated from locked video.xml while retaining the raw numeric value. Unknown/future numeric levels remain explicit unknown raw evidence rather than fabricated enum names.
- Video format enumeration remains intentionally bounded to the pre-existing sampled profile/usage recipes and must stay visibly labelled sampled-profile evidence. 0.41.45 must not claim complete format coverage or create an unbounded chroma/bit-depth/profile Cartesian expansion.
- `tools/verify_video_registry_04145.py`, `tools/test_video_profile_census_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_video_registry_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The source verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.44 and pass against 0.41.45. Negative mutations must reject source-hash drift, missing canonical profiles, StdVideo numeric drift, missing H.264 layout/AV1 film-grain products and generic-error-to-Unsupported fabrication; an unrelated text mutation is the false-positive control.
- Existing 302 Android-queryable provider extensions, 110 implemented physical-device structs, 104 validated query groups, Vulkan Profiles contracts, Surface/driver lifecycle, UUID/property serialization, schemas, endpoint/report hashing, security/privacy/resource bounds and all prior evidence-state rules remain unchanged unless separately demonstrated and contracted.
- Android release build and real-device Vulkan Video census execution are separate evidence classes and remain NOT EXECUTED unless actually run.

## Release 0.41.46 Material 3 Expressive information-architecture requirements
- Application version is 0.41.46 with versionCode 456. Vulkan registry/header baseline remains 1.4.361 / header 361; submission schema 2, technicalReport schema 3, Android compile/target API 37 and all native query coverage remain unchanged.
- Treat VulkanScope 0.41.45 as immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `0c23606857404475db3b551c9d458d1c57a05ea59c2f2f6da8b0ba5c47bfc993` and extracted predecessor census is 257 files.
- This release is UI/information-architecture only. It must not alter Vulkan capability queries, exact-profile Video census, report serialization, Database payload/hash semantics, registry/profile locks, driver/Surface lifecycle, privacy boundaries or runtime network permissions.
- Vulkan Video receives a dedicated `Vulkan Video` page driven only by existing `Vulkan Video` detailed-property and queue evidence. It organizes Overview, Decode, Encode, Formats and Queue support without inferring codec-wide support from the exact 4:2:0 8-bit query slice. Not applicable, Unknown, Unavailable, Unsupported and Supported remain distinct textual evidence states.
- The dedicated Vulkan Video page may derive a page-level `Available` presentation state only when retained exact-profile evidence rows exist and no explicit query-status failure overrides them. Explicit native `Not applicable`, `Unknown` or `Unavailable` evidence always wins; this page-level state is query/evidence availability only and must never be presented as codec support.

- Every top-level scrollable application page uses one common lazy-page wrapper with a `LazyListState`. An up indicator is visible only when `canScrollBackward` is true; a down indicator is visible only when `canScrollForward` is true. At the absolute top the up indicator is absent; at the absolute bottom the down indicator is absent; a non-scrollable page shows neither. Indicators are local Material-consistent presentation and must not change scroll state or capability data.
- Large capability collections remain lazy. The Analysis relocation must not place unbounded diff/profile/dependency/watch/test collections inside an eager nested Column or an unbounded nested vertical scroller.
- Info contains a dedicated `Libraries` section listing exact release-pinned direct application/native dependency identities used by the build: AndroidX Core KTX 1.19.0, AndroidX Activity Compose 1.13.0, Compose UI/Foundation/Animation 1.12.0, Material 3 1.5.0-alpha27, Lifecycle Runtime Compose 2.11.0, OkHttp 5.5.0, ZXing Core 3.5.4, Vulkan-Headers 1.4.361 at commit `31386378257ac8653ce5b32c93baec385259ebbe`, and arm64-only libadrenotools at commit `8fae8ce254dfc1344527e05301e43f37dea2df80`. Build-tool versions may be presented separately and must not be mislabeled as runtime libraries.
- `tools/verify_ui_information_architecture_04146.py`, `tools/test_scroll_boundary_indicators_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_ui_information_architecture_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.45 and pass against 0.41.46. Negative mutations must reject Analysis-route restoration, Analysis-before-snapshot placement, missing page-wrapper adoption, reversed arrow conditions, missing Vulkan Video destination and library pin drift, with an unrelated descriptive-text false-positive control.
- Existing 302 Android-queryable provider extensions, 110 implemented physical-device structs, 104 validated query groups, Vulkan Profiles evaluation, 47-query bounded Vulkan Video census, Surface/driver lifecycle, UUID/property serialization, endpoint/security/privacy/resource bounds and all prior release contracts remain unchanged.
- Android build/lint and real-device visual/runtime validation are separate evidence classes and remain `NOT EXECUTED` unless actually run.


## Release 0.80.0 full security, memory, specification and design audit requirements
- Application version is 0.80.0 with versionCode 800. Immutable predecessor is `VulkanScope-0.41.46.zip`, SHA-256 `b04fd07c135182010cc2237f9a0decc753cdae42abc6aed4b10bf9149da6a235`, with 265 files. Version-only metadata does not authorize any production behavior drift.
- The 2026-09-03 upstream recheck retains Vulkan API Registry/specification 1.4.361 and the independently locked Vulkan-Headers header version 361. Android compile/target API 37 and NDK r29 `29.0.14206865` remain current validated targets. AGP 9.4.0 exists, but this release retains the already validated AGP 9.3.2 / Gradle 9.7.1 / built-in Kotlin + Compose compiler configuration because no specification, security or correctness defect requires a toolchain migration.
- User-selected Turnip/AdrenoTools archives remain untrusted input. Raw archive bytes consumed from the selected content URI must be bounded to 96 MiB before `ZipInputStream`, in addition to the existing 2048-entry, 32 MiB per-file and 64 MiB decompressed-total limits. Exceeding any bound fails closed and temporary private extraction state is deleted.
- Normal probe polling may observe publication file metadata, but it must not repeatedly read and build a full JSON object tree for each changed intermediate checkpoint. Full publication bytes are read only at service-owned terminal handoff or bounded crash/timeout recovery boundaries. Terminal JSON validation in the main process must be strict and streaming; missing `status` is not terminal evidence and must never default to fabricated `unavailable`. The service remains the sole normal `.done` publisher and retains its strict streaming validator.
- The 64 MiB probe publication cap, 60-second background detail budget, serialization mutex, cancellation teardown, stale-process barrier, process-owned native cleanup, Surface generation gates, report-completeness rules, exact Unknown/Unavailable/Unsupported/Not applicable distinctions, Database 2 MiB fail-closed complete-payload ceiling and all previous concurrency/resource contracts remain mandatory.
- Security review must retain `allowBackup=false`, cleartext disabled, non-exported FileProvider/probe service, cache-confined update packages, official HTTPS GitHub release provenance, APK package/signing/version validation, fixed HTTPS Database root with redirects disabled, explicit Database submission, private Turnip storage, canonical ZIP path confinement, duplicate-path rejection and native stack-protector/RELRO/NOW hardening.
- Vulkan 1.4.361 registry/header/query coverage, 302 Android-queryable provider extensions, 110 implemented physical-device structures, 104 validated query groups and the 47-query Vulkan Video exact-profile census are unchanged unless a separately demonstrated specification delta exists. GPU names, Android versions or marketing names never substitute for runtime capability evidence.
- The 0.41.46 Material 3 Expressive architecture is preserved: Vulkan Video remains a dedicated destination; Analysis remains embedded directly below Capability snapshot; top-level scrollable pages use the common boundary-aware `LazyListState` wrapper; Info retains exact Libraries/build-toolchain identities. No security/memory fix may hide, truncate or ellipsize canonical Vulkan evidence.
- `tools/verify_full_hardening_0800.py`, `tools/test_full_hardening_0800_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_full_hardening_0800_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The source verifier must fail against immutable 0.41.46 and pass against 0.80.0. Negative mutations must reject raw-archive bound removal/drift, terminal-validation bypass and renewed intermediate full-publication reads, while an unrelated UI text mutation must pass as a false-positive control.
- `tests/golden/0.41.46_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract for 0.80.0. Production changes are restricted to the demonstrated security/memory/performance fix in `MainActivity.kt` plus version metadata. All other production bytes, registry/query/schema/native/UI semantics and normalized build configuration are locked.
- Android Gradle assemble/lint/unit, real-device Vulkan execution, HWASan/ASan/LeakCanary/Perfetto and vendor-driver stress remain separate evidence classes. They may be reported PASS only if actually executed. Static ownership/resource review and source/state-machine gates do not prove absence of all vendor-driver leaks or crashes.

## Release 0.80.1 Overview Encyclopedia requirements
- Application version is 0.80.1 with versionCode 801. Immutable predecessor is `VulkanScope-0.80.0.zip`, SHA-256 `09ae1a26347f906203c1992c78ac61ab119d0ccbf591d99848f04b36f158ed51`, with 270 files.

It must state that Vulkan Video being Not applicable describes the Vulkan Video API path exposed by the selected Vulkan driver and does not prove absence of hardware video decode/encode capability.

## Release 0.80.2 local Vulkan encyclopedia expansion
- VulkanScope 0.80.1 is the immutable predecessor. The Overview Encyclopedia remains between Capability snapshot and Analysis workspace.
- The Encyclopedia is a local/offline reference, not runtime capability evidence. It must not add a network path or infer support from registry registration, symbol presence, a GPU name, or a driver name.
- The Encyclopedia must explain VulkanScope evidence states plus Vulkan naming classes (`vk*` commands, `vkCmd*` command-buffer recording commands, `vkQueue*` queue commands, `Vk*` types/handles/structures, and `VK_*` enums/constants/bit flags/extension names).
- The complete locked Vulkan 1.4.361 registry symbol census must be searchable locally for registered `vk*` commands, `VK_*` tokens/enumerants and `Vk*` types. Large symbol families must remain bounded in visible results and must not create a nested vertical scroller or eagerly materialize the full registry symbol universe on every recomposition.
- `VkResult` receives a dedicated authoritative glossary. Canonical result names, numeric values and meanings must match the locked/published Vulkan 1.4.361 specification. Alias names remain discoverable but do not replace canonical reporting names.
- The Encyclopedia must explain common VulkanScope query commands and core concepts such as instance, physical device, logical device, queue, command buffer, feature, property, limit, format, layer, extension, Surface/WSI, swapchain, `pNext`, `sType`, API version, loader version and driver version.
- The prior Encyclopedia destination shortcut chips/buttons for Extensions, Profiles and Vulkan Video are removed. Search/category controls are allowed; no destination navigation buttons remain inside the Encyclopedia.
- Registry symbol ownership/provider metadata is reference metadata only and is never presented as proof that the selected runtime exposes or supports the symbol.
- The generated symbol index is produced only at build/release time from the checked-in locked registry and is checked in. Runtime registry download/parsing is forbidden.
- `tools/verify_encyclopedia_0802.py`, its failing-before-fix predecessor run, registry-symbol generator equality check and negative-mutation suite are mandatory.
- Android assemble/lint/unit and real-device visual validation remain separate evidence classes and are NOT EXECUTED unless actually run.

## Release 0.80.3 separate Overview tools and crash-safe Encyclopedia search
- Application version is 0.80.3 with versionCode 803. Immutable predecessor is `VulkanScope-0.80.2.zip`, SHA-256 `be11ceea517c1a36f3720ebed866624a754b8b2f8d2584bfe6db59ee477b04e5`, with 280 files.
- This user-requested information-architecture change supersedes the 0.80.1/0.80.2 requirement that the complete Encyclopedia and Analysis workspace be embedded inside Overview. Overview now contains only compact Material 3 Expressive destination cards for `Encyclopedia` and `Analysis workspace`; selecting either opens a separate page with the existing app-header back behavior, while the bottom/rail navigation continues to identify Overview as the parent destination.
- Overview must not instantiate `rememberAnalysisWorkspaceModel`, compute Analysis-only diff/profile/graph/watch/self-test state, execute Encyclopedia search, or eagerly compose either full tool surface. Analysis-only work begins only when `Page.Analysis` is active. Encyclopedia search/index work begins only when `Page.Encyclopedia` is active.
- Both separate tool pages use the common `VulkanLazyPage`/`LazyListState` wrapper and therefore retain the established upper/lower scroll-boundary indicators. Encyclopedia result entries are independent lazy-list items rather than a bounded result list eagerly composed inside one large `Column`.
- The 0.80.2 search crash is a correctness regression in the generated registry-symbol encoding contract: generated Kotlin rows contained literal `\\t` text while `decodeVulkanRegistrySymbol` searched for runtime tab characters. A matching search row could therefore reach `substring` with delimiter index `-1` and throw `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException`. The generator must emit exactly one Kotlin `\t` escape per field boundary so the runtime string contains a tab.
- Generated symbol decoding is fail-closed. `decodeVulkanRegistrySymbol` returns no entry for a row without two valid non-empty tab-delimited fields, and the search loop skips malformed rows before substring/decoding. A malformed checked-in/generated row must never crash search or navigation.
- The locked Vulkan 1.4.361 symbol census, authoritative VkResult meanings, 24-result visible bound, offline/no-network reference model, runtime-vs-registry evidence separation, Vulkan Video Not applicable clarification, 302 provider-extension / 110 struct / 104 query-group coverage, 47-query Vulkan Video census, security/resource/report contracts and Database schemas remain unchanged.
- `tools/verify_overview_tools_search_0803.py`, `tools/test_encyclopedia_search_0803_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_overview_tools_search_0803_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.80.2 and pass against 0.80.3. Negative mutations must reject re-embedding Analysis in Overview, route removal, double-escaped symbol separators, decoder/search malformed-row guard removal and generator drift; an unrelated permitted Overview description mutation is the false-positive control.
- `tests/golden/0.80.2_regression_contract.json` is the immutable predecessor contract. Production changes are restricted to the user-requested navigation/lazy-layout repair in `MainActivity.kt`, the crash repair in generated `VulkanSymbolIndex.kt`, and version metadata. Generator/verifier/rules/test changes are release tooling and do not authorize unrelated runtime drift.
- Android assemble/lint/unit and real-device UI/search validation remain separate evidence classes and may be reported PASS only if actually executed.

## Release 0.80.4 detail affordance, scroll/TV and collection-outcome requirements
- Application version is 0.80.4 with versionCode 804. Immutable predecessor is `VulkanScope-0.80.3.zip`, SHA-256 `04a1c45804d1d1ecc13f1b5cf70729d42e551313be091540f3f981cac5c73807`, with 285 files.
- Format and Extension entries that open detailed evidence must expose a visible Material 3 Expressive `Details` affordance while preserving the existing whole-card activation and read-only evidence semantics.
- Detail dialogs with potentially overflowing evidence use bounded vertical scrolling and the same upper/lower boundary semantics as top-level pages: Up is hidden at the top, Down is hidden at the bottom, and both are hidden when content does not scroll.
- Extensions and Formats remain standard top-level `LazyColumn` pages. Touch/user scrolling is enabled; no nested vertical lazy list or gesture-consuming custom key layer may be introduced.
- Android TV navigation uses standard Compose Foundation lazy focus-driven scrolling. Every detail-capable Format/Extension card and read-only modal evidence target must be focusable on TV and request BringIntoView when focused. D-pad focus must not imply mutability, and Enter/center activation may only invoke an already-clickable detail destination.
- The compact collection-in-progress status surface retains its activity icon but does not display a `Collecting information...` label inside that chip. Adjacent explanatory status copy is permitted.
- Collection completion has separate success and failure states. A null report, fatal collection error/timeout, `baseReportComplete=false`, zero physical devices, or an all-unknown/all-empty device result must show a failure X and `Failed`; it must never reuse the successful `Completed` presentation.
- A valid complete base report remains globally successful even when optional/deferred query groups individually report Unknown, Unavailable or Not applicable. Existing query-state evidence must not be flattened into global failure or Unsupported.
- `tools/verify_detail_tv_collection_0804.py`, `tools/test_detail_tv_collection_0804_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_detail_tv_collection_0804_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. ## Release 0.80.5 update release-notes Android TV and design-integrity requirements

- Update presentation must retain the Material 3 Expressive hierarchy: 24dp raised update banner, 32dp update dialog, matching 20dp metadata/release-notes inner surfaces, rose/red focus treatment and existing Review / Download APK / Cancel action hierarchy.
- Release-note markdown meaning and typography hierarchy must not be changed merely to add focus. The local focus wrapper must be presentation-only and must not imply clickability, capability support or mutable state.

## Release 0.80.6 TalkBack and large-text/display-size accessibility requirements
- Application version is 0.80.6 with versionCode 806. Immutable predecessor is VulkanScope 0.80.5 with ZIP SHA-256 `92a2ea2e853c6b01a65e1a2076d737c8e9595b06b3db28b3ee5e86a0ae538a43` and package census 296 files.
- Android accessibility guidance is authoritative. TalkBack semantics must describe purpose/state without duplicate decorative announcements. Decorative scroll-boundary arrows, repeated navigation icons, vendor artwork and chevrons must not create redundant accessibility nodes when adjacent text or parent controls already provide the meaning.
- Dynamic collection and update status surfaces use Compose accessibility live-region semantics. Collection status is one merged read-only announcement unit and must preserve the distinct Collecting, Completed and Failed meanings introduced in 0.80.4.
- Section titles and release-note markdown headings are accessibility headings. Read-only key/value surfaces merge their key and value into one coherent semantics unit without implying clickability.
- Direct GitHub updates are exposed as one Switch semantic target. The visual nested Switch must not add a second action. Vulkan driver options are exposed as one RadioButton semantic target and the nested visual RadioButton must not add a duplicate action.
- Android 14+ nonlinear font scaling up to 200% and enlarged display-size settings must not rely on fixed-height text containers. Large-text/narrow-layout mode is presentation-only and may use `Configuration.fontScale` only as a layout threshold, never as a manual sp-to-dp conversion formula.
- Large-text/narrow-layout mode activates at fontScale >= 1.3 or screenWidthDp < 360. It stacks ordinary key/value rows and update metadata, reduces Overview Quick Access from four to two columns, stacks Overview metric pairs, Info metadata pills and paired export actions, widens the scrollable navigation rail and allows rail labels/card heights to grow with natural content height.
- Quick Access and navigation-rail items use minimum heights rather than fixed heights. Collection status copy may wrap. The large-text app header replaces the fixed logo-plus-page-title geometry with one scalable textual title so the current destination remains visible without overlap.
- The update dialog retains the normal 360dp release-notes ceiling and uses a smaller bounded 220dp release-notes viewport in large-text/narrow-layout mode so metadata, actions and explanatory text retain room. Release-note scrolling, TV focus/BringIntoView and boundary indicators remain unchanged.
- Material 3 Expressive colors, shapes, hierarchy and capability semantics remain unchanged. Accessibility/layout adaptation must not infer Vulkan support, alter report data, change Database payloads, change runtime network behavior or weaken TV focus/navigation contracts.
- `tools/verify_accessibility_large_text_0806.py`, `tools/test_accessibility_large_text_0806_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_accessibility_large_text_0806_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The source verifier must fail against immutable 0.80.5 with version checking disabled and pass on 0.80.6. Negative mutations must reject semantic duplication, live-region removal, fixed-height restoration, large-text reflow removal and adaptive update-dialog viewport removal, while an unrelated permitted text change remains a false-positive control.
- Android TalkBack traversal, Accessibility Scanner/instrumented semantics tests, 200% font-size visual testing, enlarged display-size testing, portrait/landscape device validation and Android build/lint/unit remain separate evidence classes and may be called PASS only if actually executed.

## Release 0.80.7 system-language, bidi, system-font and update-information requirements
- Application version is 0.80.7 with versionCode 807. Immutable predecessor is VulkanScope 0.80.6 with ZIP SHA-256 `8cb67397f76ba5a1a1ada0c5d8f1046895161d754a9a2f5654da4ce52b9f9b69` and package census 301 files.
- Android RTL and Compose text-direction guidance are authoritative. `android:supportsRtl="true"` remains enabled. Layout continues to use relative Start/End behavior; application UI code must not introduce absolute `TextAlign.Left` / `TextAlign.Right` for ordinary presentation.
- The application remains English-only unless a separately audited localization release adds translated resources. System locale must nevertheless not corrupt Latin/Vulkan technical identifiers, punctuation or mixed-script text. Material typography uses content-aware `TextDirection.ContentOrLtr`: Arabic/Hebrew content may resolve RTL, while Latin/Cyrillic/Vulkan identifiers remain LTR even when the Android layout direction is RTL.

- Unicode text is preserved as UTF-8. UI/report strings must not be ASCII-filtered merely to make layout deterministic. - The update-available banner uses a distinct blue information state: a circular `ic_info` glyph with blue container/tint appears in the same visual status position used by collection success/failure icons. It is decorative to TalkBack because the live-region update text is authoritative. The old green `UPDATE` badge is not shown for the Available state.
- The blue update-information state is presentation only. It must not change update discovery, provenance validation, APK download rules, signing/package/version validation, network endpoints or user confirmation requirements.
- `tools/verify_system_language_font_update_0807.py`, `tools/test_system_language_font_update_0807_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_system_language_font_update_0807_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.80.6 with version checking disabled and pass on 0.80.7. Negative mutations must reject RTL disablement, content-aware direction removal, font-family hard-coding, blue Info removal/color drift and old UPDATE badge restoration while accepting an unrelated permitted wording change.

## Release 0.80.10 Vulkan 1.4.362 registry/header requirements
- Application version is 0.80.10 with versionCode 810. Treat VulkanScope 0.80.9 as immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `48bc7d2d3e5aae3cb69437dc620d58191b266f341dcab3050f10180ed5c5f172` with 316 files.

The bundled `registry/upstream/vk.xml` SHA-256 is `cf31c965cf6e788697139601da0c7e02a75a9b6c7ac764e7641f5521ffd9da06`; canonical Vulkan-Headers is pinned to tag v1.4.362 and commit `ee2ec5fd83dafce291024683b50dc89219333076`. The published specification is Vulkan 1.4.362 dated 2026-09-04.
- Registry-driven Android-queryable physical-device provider coverage is exactly 304 extensions: 299 stable plus the existing 5 provisional providers. The 1.4.362 delta must include `VK_KHR_pipeline_library_group_handles` and `VK_VALVE_buffer_device_address_allocation_alignment`.
- The explicit native query catalog remains exactly 110 implemented physical-device structs and 104 validated query groups. New 1.4.362 provider structs are handled through the validated generic extension pNext/serializer path and must not create an ad-hoc duplicate query group.
- `VK_EXT_pipeline_library_group_handles` and promoted `VK_KHR_pipeline_library_group_handles` must share alias-equivalent canonical KHR structure coverage. The pNext generator must resolve registry type aliases so both providers remain schedulable after promotion.
- `VK_VALVE_buffer_device_address_allocation_alignment` must query both `VkPhysicalDeviceBufferDeviceAddressAllocationAlignmentFeaturesVALVE` and `VkPhysicalDeviceBufferDeviceAddressAllocationAlignmentPropertiesVALVE`, and report `bufferDeviceAddressAllocationAlignment` plus `maxBufferDeviceAddressAllocationAlignment` without inferring support from registry presence.
- The complete Vulkan extension reference census is 476. The local Encyclopedia corpus generated from the locked 1.4.362 registry is exactly 842 vk* commands, 6248 VK_* tokens, 2461 Vk* types, 476 extensions and 50 canonical VkResult entries. Registry/reference presence remains separate from runtime capability evidence.
- Vulkan Video remains the locked bounded 47 exact-profile census. Updating vk.xml provenance must regenerate the checked-in video registry header; no codec-wide, bit-depth-wide or hardware-video inference may be introduced.
- Submission schema 2, technicalReport 3, Database endpoint/security/privacy/resource ceilings, Turnip hardening, report state semantics, TalkBack/TV/large-text/RTL behavior and explicit Details-button-only interaction remain unchanged unless separately demonstrated and contracted.
- Companion Database is 0.39.24 and uses the same locked Vulkan 1.4.362 Encyclopedia/reference corpus. Its new-submission floor remains VulkanScope 0.80.3.
- `tools/verify_vulkan_1_4_362_0810.py` and `tools/test_vulkan_1_4_362_0810_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.80.9 with version checking disabled and pass on 0.80.10. The mutation suite must reject registry-ref drift, missing new provider coverage, promoted-alias loss, VALVE field-reporting loss and Encyclopedia census drift while accepting an unrelated wording-only change as a false-positive control. The immutable regression contract, retained release gates, deterministic ZIP creation, source-to-extract byte equality and extracted-package verification remain mandatory.
- Android assemble/lint/unit, real-device execution and sanitizer/profiler runs are separate evidence classes and are NOT EXECUTED unless actually run.

## Release 0.80.13 Material 3 Expressive full-UI coherence requirements
- Application version is 0.80.13 with versionCode 813. VulkanScope 0.80.12 is the immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `8915b55cccd846b3b62d2359a9fa5c2119d702ae2c70d8f26fd17697362b7536` with 324 files.
- This user-requested full-UI presentation pass supersedes earlier geometry freezes only where necessary to correct demonstrated Material 3 Expressive consistency/usability defects. Information architecture, destination order, Vulkan branding, dark-neutral palette, #A41E22/#E2676A identity, explicit Details-only activation, TV browse semantics and capability evidence semantics remain authoritative.
- Official Android Material 3 guidance rechecked for this release identifies Material 3 Expressive as the current M3 evolution, recommends theme-driven color/typography/shape systems, and recommends custom `Dialog` content when a dialog requires a complex freeform layout rather than forcing complex content through `AlertDialog` slots. Material 3 remains pinned to 1.5.0-alpha27; its current `Shapes` API includes `largeIncreased`, `extraLargeIncreased` and `extraExtraLarge`.
- `MaterialExpressiveTheme` with `MotionScheme.expressive()` remains the application theme. The Vulkan shape system must populate the complete alpha27 shape scale and major shared card/modal surfaces use that theme scale instead of unrelated local radii where a matching semantic shape exists.
- Long Format/Extension detail content uses a custom Material-themed `Dialog` with a responsive maximum width, display-height-aware bounded body, clear title/body/action separation and the existing explicit Close action. It must remain user-scrollable and retain real `canScroll`/scroll-range boundary indicators.
- Detail-dialog key/value evidence must not be rendered as a column of competing nested bubble cards. Inside detail dialogs the shared key/value component uses a flat transparent row with a subtle divider; outside dialogs it retains the established tonal read-only surface and TV browse/focus semantics.
- Key/value layout is width/content aware. Long keys, long values, multiline values, large text and constrained widths stack label above value. Two-column rows are permitted only when content is short enough to remain readable; values are not right-aligned into ragged multi-line blocks.
- Top-level lazy pages and bounded detail/release-note scroll areas reserve a visual edge lane for boundary indicators so indicators do not cover canonical Vulkan text. Up/down visibility remains driven only by actual scroll boundaries and decorative arrows remain excluded from accessibility announcements.
- The explicit `Details` action uses the shared Material 3 Expressive morphing-button shape behavior and retains explicit `Role.Button` semantics. Surrounding Format/Extension cards remain read-only and must never regain whole-card detail activation.
- Format and Extension list rows use the common capability-item hierarchy. Analysis uses the shared expressive Switch wrapper; no direct standalone Material Switch is permitted outside that wrapper.
- Startup collection and ordinary empty states use the same Material 3 Expressive surface/icon/typography hierarchy as loaded pages. Startup progress is indeterminate and must not fabricate completion percentages or capability states.
- Action-card titles/subtitles may grow to bounded multi-line text so essential actions such as Database submission are not ellipsized merely because of phone width or system text scaling.
- Accessibility, large-text, RTL/system-font, Android TV focus/BringIntoView, update release-note lazy scrolling, report/export, update security and network/privacy contracts remain mandatory. Presentation changes must not alter raw Vulkan evidence, query scheduling, native code, report serialization, Database payloads, endpoint selection or capability classification.
- Companion Database is 0.39.27. Submission remains schema 2 / technicalReport 3 and no D1 migration or report rewrite is introduced by this application UI release.
- `tools/verify_material3_expressive_ui_0813.py`, `tools/test_material3_expressive_ui_0813_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_material3_expressive_ui_0813_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.80.12 with version checking disabled and pass on 0.80.13. Negative mutations must reject return to complex AlertDialog slots, cramped key/value thresholds, indicator/content overlap, boxed detail rows, direct Analysis Switch use, unstyled startup loading and stale release identity; an unrelated documentation wording change is the false-positive control.
- `tests/golden/0.80.12_regression_contract.json` locks predecessor production/build-chain bytes. Production runtime changes are limited to `MainActivity.kt` presentation behavior plus version metadata in `app/build.gradle.kts`; Vulkan/native/registry/generated/report/security/resource files remain byte-identical to 0.80.12.
- Android assemble/lint/unit, real-device portrait/landscape rendering, TalkBack, Android TV D-pad traversal and original-device visual reproduction remain separate evidence classes and may be called PASS only when actually executed.

## Release 0.80.15 responsive overlay-scroll and detail-dialog requirements
- Application version is 0.80.15 with versionCode 815. VulkanScope 0.80.14 is the immutable predecessor; predecessor ZIP SHA-256 is `fdfeb5937bc51264854f224ad43fc0571430f398932b876fbce1fd688610b315` with 333 files.
- The supplied real-device screenshots are the USABILITY/REGRESSION oracle. They demonstrate that the 0.80.13 reserved right-side scroll-indicator lane visibly narrows Overview content, horizontally clipped Explore controls remain awkward on phone widths, and the 0.80.13 flat detail-dialog list is less readable than the earlier grouped evidence presentation.
- This release explicitly supersedes only the 0.80.13 requirements that reserved a permanent edge lane for scroll indicators and required flat transparent detail-dialog key/value rows. All other 0.80.13 Material 3 Expressive, accessibility, Details-only, evidence, TV, report, security and privacy requirements remain mandatory.
- Shared scroll-boundary indicators overlay content rather than permanently consuming horizontal layout width. Decorative indicators never alter canonical Vulkan text and retain null content descriptions.
- Boundary direction is derived exclusively from actual scrollability: absolute top shows only Down, middle positions show Up and Down, absolute bottom shows only Up, and non-scrollable content shows neither.
- The indicator surfaces and arrows are larger than 0.80.14 while remaining visually subordinate to content. Up is overlaid at the viewport top-center and Down at the viewport bottom-center rather than in a side lane. Visibility is activity-aware: indicators appear while gesture/fling/programmatic scrolling is in progress, remain briefly visible after movement, then fade out; renewed scrolling makes them fade back in. Initial scrollable content may briefly show the applicable boundary hint before the same idle timeout.
- The shared arrow icon geometry is 24dp inside a compact Material 3 Expressive tonal surface; indicators use fade-only visibility changes and do not slide after scrolling stops.
- Top-level LazyColumn pages, bounded detail dialogs and bounded update release notes use the same overlay indicator behavior. No scroll area reserves a fixed right-side lane merely for the indicator.
- Detail dialogs keep the custom Material-themed Dialog architecture but return to grouped evidence cards. Detail key/value cards use the shared tonal surface, subtle outline and responsive one-column/two-column layout. Long values, long labels, large system text, narrow portrait widths and translated strings stack label above value rather than forcing ragged or clipped columns.
- Detail dialog width, horizontal/vertical margins and body height remain bounded from current Configuration dimensions so phone/tablet, portrait/landscape and display-size/font-scale changes cannot push the sticky Close action outside the usable modal surface.
- Overview Explore and Quick access controls must not depend on a clipped horizontal chip strip or fixed four-column phone grid. They use width-aware wrapping grids that reduce column count for narrow or enlarged-text layouts while preserving every destination.
- MaterialExpressiveTheme, MotionScheme.expressive(), Vulkan dark-neutral + #A41E22/#E2676A visual identity, shape scale, Details-only activation, TV focus/BringIntoView, TalkBack, RTL/system-font and large-text contracts remain unchanged.
- Vulkan 1.4.362/header 362, 304 provider extensions, 110 implemented structs, 104 query groups, 47 Vulkan Video exact-profile combinations, Encyclopedia census, submission schema 2, technicalReport 3, Database 0.39.27 compatibility, report serialization, native query behavior, capability classification and security/privacy/resource ceilings are unchanged.
- `tools/verify_responsive_overlay_ui_0815.py`, `tools/test_responsive_overlay_ui_0815_state_machine.py` and `tools/test_responsive_overlay_ui_0815_negative_mutations.py` are mandatory. The verifier must fail against immutable 0.80.14 with version checking disabled and pass on 0.80.15. Negative mutations must reject restoration of the reserved indicator lane, loss of activity-aware fade behavior, smaller arrows, wrong boundary direction, flat detail rows, loose detail stacking, horizontally scrolling Explore controls, fixed four-column Quick access and stale release identity; an unrelated changelog wording mutation is the false-positive control.
- `tests/golden/0.80.14_regression_contract.json` locks the immutable predecessor. Production runtime changes are limited to `MainActivity.kt` presentation/scroll behavior plus version metadata in `app/build.gradle.kts`; Vulkan/native/registry/generated/report/security/resource files remain byte-identical to 0.80.14.
- Android assemble/lint/unit, real-device portrait/landscape reproduction, TalkBack, Android TV D-pad traversal and device-specific visual confirmation are separate evidence classes and remain NOT EXECUTED unless actually run after this release.
